Portolan — progress

The single source of truth for the state of the work. Spec: PROMPT.md. Screenshots: progress/shots/. Last updated: 2026-10-24 (WP5; WP18 part A; WP6 parts A and B).

Agent protocol (read first)

  1. Read this page top to bottom, then PROMPT.md in full. PROMPT.md is the spec; every line of it is required. Nothing here may weaken it.
  2. Pick the work package (WP) you were assigned in Work packages. Check its dependencies are done. Set its status line to Status: in progress (YYYY-MM-DD).
  3. Touch only the files your WP owns, plus the append-only registration lines in the shared files. Another agent may be working in the same checkout: re-read a shared file immediately before editing it, never reformat files you do not own, and stage only your own paths (git add <paths>, never git add -A).
  4. Legacy code from the earlier game is reference material only. It is deleted from the tree in WP1; read it with git show 6ce29f2:src/<file>.ts (see Code audit for what is worth adapting). Never re-add src/assets/ or any image, model, font or audio file.
  5. Before finishing: npm run typecheck, npm test, and the checks/captures named in your WP's definition of done; the console must be free of errors and warnings. Save your evidence as progress/shots/wpNN-*.png (before and after where relevant).
  6. Update this page: your WP status (done (date) with a one-line result), the spec status rows you advanced, acceptance check results, captures, a dated log entry with before/after screenshots, and what comes next. Keep every section's id stable; edit with text tools; keep one table row per line.
  7. Commit on main with message WPnn: <title> and the trailer line Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>. List any new library in CREDITS.md with source and licence.
  8. Code standard (PROMPT.md "How to work"): every line earns its place; no dead code, duplication, speculative features or placeholder art left behind; create files only when needed; prefer the carefully built procedural solution over the shortcut.
  9. Art direction from the user (2026-10-24): the environment must be more realistic and far richer in detail than the first terrain/sea passes, while keeping the drawn aesthetic. Think of a master engraver/illustrator who draws from life: believable geology and proportions (eroded headlands, gullies, crags, boulders, scree, pebbled and sandy beaches, cliff strata with fissures, varied silhouettes rather than uniform mesas), believable water (dense, varied wave strokes, swell, reflections, foam patterns), dense believable vegetation, and many layers of fine detail at every distance — all expressed through ink line, hatching and transparent watercolour, never photoreal PBR. Every render package and every review applies this.
  10. Shared working copy: never rebuild or revert progress.html (or any shared file) from HEAD — that drops other agents' uncommitted edits. Edit only your own lines with targeted replacements, re-reading them first. Keep scratch files in a subdirectory of the scratchpad unique to your package (e.g. scratchpad/wp16a/).
  11. Priorities from the user (2026-10-25): graphics and gameplay quality come before strict performance requirements. Record triangle counts, draw calls and frame times for the later performance pass (WP30), but never cut visible quality to meet a budget before then, and never add a failing budget assert for it.

2026-10-25: WP17 parts B–F (the other five regions) on the coralAtolls.ts/WP16 pattern; Coral Atolls follow-ups in WP17’s Remaining (reef-flat ground and reef-front walls are terrain.ts’s, coral tufts flora.ts’s).

WP26 done (part C 693b7b1): the 3D harbour landed; leftovers in its Remaining (fittings at the mooring, structures reach by quality).

State of each PROMPT.md section

Status values: done (meets the spec, verified) · in progress · not started. Nothing from the earlier game counts as done: it was a different game in a different (photoreal) style.

Simulation: WP6. WP6 part A: one swirling planetary wind shifting slowly (≤ 0.7°/s where settled, ~54° over a match) with gusts running downwind; ±15 % by point of sail; true and apparent wind exported; the lanes and the Great Current carry ships. WP6 part B: the tide opens and shuts the Amber Shoals' and Atolls' passages on its 120 s rhythm (tideOf, untilTide); 48 weather spells from the seed — fog banks, squalls (wind ×1.5 and gusty, sea ×1.8, sight ×0.55), calms (wind ×0.25, a flat sea) — drift with the wind and form by the regions' odds, squalls 3.5× likelier on the open sea; exported for horizon, chart and sight (state.weather, skyAt, veiled, inFog); the hour starts at dawn, afternoon or the blue hour by the seed and advances 3.5 h in ten minutes (hourOf, sunDir). Drawing them: WP13, WP14, WP21.

A match › The stormin progressWP8, WP21WP8 part C (2026-10-24): origin anywhere; seven phases announced, swept and held; nested calms toward a final calm uniform over the sphere (χ² 7.96 on 11 df, 10 000 seeds); hull loss rising each phase, sails labour without storm sails, sight drops, held sea swallowed; the last phase closes the final calm by 600 s. Combat (B) and the storm (C) call it. Part C: the storm ends every match (its last phase closes the final calm), replacing the 720 s deadline; ships it sinks together go least sea first.

Review findings

Each review is by a fresh reviewer agent (see WP33). Record every finding as one row: id (R1-001…), severity (blocker / major / minor), where (capture, screen, or system), the flaw, fix WP, fix commit, status. A finding is closed only when the fix is verified by a new capture or test.

Orchestrator spot checks (pre-review; fix in the owning WP or the first fix round)

  • S-001 (wp20-flood-0-open, high camera): a low cloud floats mid-frame over the action and hides the trail — clouds must never sit between a gameplay camera and the ship/trail (fade or lift clouds near the camera's line of sight). — fixed by WP21 (27e4e96): clouds near the line of sight from the camera down to the sea in view slide clear of it (sky.ts cloud vertex shader); clouds toward the horizon stay. Verified 2026-10-24 on wp21-sea-* (WP21b bdf4412).
  • S-002 (same frame): the open sea reads as large blotchy light/dark camouflage patches — the depth wash / pigment-load unevenness is too coarse and too contrasty; it should read as delicate depth washes with fine variation (WP13 owner). — fixed by WP21 (27e4e96): the depth washes take 30 % of the paper's mottle and bloom, the swell's tone range narrows to 0.88–1.14 and the deep contour wavers less (sea.ts); not yet re-shot on the wp20 frame. Re-shot 2026-10-24 (wp21-sea-*, WP21b bdf4412): still reads as large pale/deep patches — open.
  • S-003 (same frame): dark smudges under islands look like floating shadows — check sea-floor/terrain-under-water compositing (WP13/WP15).
  • S-004: addressed (WP16e, 2026-10-24) — the Weeping Isle no longer reads as a drum from high or orbit (a cloud-forest massif and spire over a cloud girdle, falls all round, buttresses out of the band); from deck the rim still reads fairly level between faceted buttresses: an eroded buttress kit and true gullies cut in the WP15 terrain remain (terrain.ts owners).
  • S-005: the sea still uses a default wind (sim windAt wiring crashed non-match screens) — fix in WP21. — fixed by WP21 (27e4e96): render/weather.ts Air (airOf(scene)) reads the sim's wind over the eye whenever a match exists and a fair trade wind otherwise; the sea, sky clouds, rain and spray use it (tests/render/weather.test.ts). Verified in every wp21 shot (WP21b bdf4412).
  • S-006 (WP16a): neither the Crown Light nor the Great Falls reads clearly from orbit; the Great Falls is hidden from capture 18's camera; fjord water is not the deep green the spec asks for (sea depth wash per region).
  • S-007: WP9a/WP9b sim tick-budget and claim-timing tests fail when the machine is loaded (three agents running) — measure CPU time per tick, not wall time, and keep real headroom under A9 (tick mean is 0.16–0.27 ms against 0.3 ms before captains exist). Fixed (2026-10-24): every cost budget reads this thread's CPU time (tests/cpu.ts cpuNow(), process.threadCpuUsage; budgets unchanged), and npm test is tests/run.ts: the unit files in parallel, then every file that times itself or drives a browser one at a time, so they never contend with the suite (the iPhone boot failure was load: title 534 ms alone against 1500). Two full runs on a loaded machine (other agents' suites): 264/264 pass both; ten ships fighting 0.146–0.174 ms, every gull trained 0.165–0.196 ms.
  • S-008 (WP21b): a dark arc band crosses the squall and opening-bruise shots (wp21-*), cause unknown (not the two causes WP21b fixed). S-002 (blotchy open sea) also still open after WP21b re-shot it.
  • S-009 (WP16c): kelp from flora.ts reads as grass at deck level; Kelp Cathedral light rays barely visible from the hero camera. (WP16b): basalt columns look smooth at hero distance; the Arch crest does not stand out from orbit.
  • S-010 (WP16d): the Sandbar Light reads only as a small mark from orbit (needs a bolder silhouette/beam); hero shot has too much pale sky and the light is small on iPhone; low gold light reads as warm paper rather than low sun on wet sand; the set-stone line runs along the spit beach instead of straight out to sea; reflection faint and drainage channels thin at hero distance.
  • S-011 (WP19b): sinking — masts do not fall one by one (she rolls and the sails come in); floating wreckage, slick and rings lie flat and do not ride the swell; the lateen-mizzen fitting adds a second yard on forms whose mizzen is already lateen; brown blobs in the sky in the sinking frames (present before any sinking — ash from the dark island? unexplained); chart-height crossing white wakes read heavy (sea.ts wakes); damage, points-of-sail and iPhone hero sheets not yet reviewed.
  • S-012 (WP22a): from the deck camera a fight read faintly and shells looked like oversized black dots (tuned after, never re-reviewed); the heavy ten-ship battle frame reached 515k triangles, over the 500k A9 limit (FX are ~1% — the scene/ships/terrain LODs must give the headroom).
  • S-013 (WP23a + orchestrator; fixed in WP30c (f9e0a36, f3a621e): every standard view ≤420k (worst 412k); the WP23 fauna page itself was not re-measured): triangle budget breached in several frames though each new package is cheap: the heron life capture frame is 790k triangles, the deck→chart swap 741–757k (WP24c fixing), the ten-ship battle 515k. A dedicated scene-LOD package (terrain/flora/region heroes/structures by distance and screen size, culling behind the horizon) must bring every capture to ≤500k with headroom (aim ≤420k). WP23a also: trait marks too small to judge in plumage close-ups (tighter framing needed), call-to-drop and reaction sequences not captured, animals placed from region data rather than the region hooks (colonies, haul-outs, feeding spots, kelp rafts are not reachable from the scene yet).
  • S-014 (WP16e): Weeping Isle from deck falls short — cliffs 7–10 u high instead of the sim's 18 u so the rim reads level; buttresses read as faceted boxes, not eroded rock; falls are thin threads and spray bright blobs at hero distance; the massif is painted faces, not forest. The agent lowered three of its own test thresholds to fit (falls 30→26, buttresses 10→6, boulders 150→100) — restore them when the island is fixed. Its high shot is at 420k on iPad (at the limit).
  • S-015 (WP25b): touch→tick latency on iPad is 1.77 frames median (29.5 ms) against the one-frame target (iPhone 0.83 frames, 13.9 ms); suspected cause: the loop ticks before the frame's touches reach the controls. Compact chart reads sparse at match start.
  • S-016 (WP26c): the harbour gull is a separate new mesh rather than the match gull from src/render/gull.ts (the companion must be the same bird everywhere), and it reads small on iPad; ship fittings are not shown in the harbour (career holds none between matches — confirm against PROMPT); lighthouse/harbour detail in structures.ts ignores the graphics-quality setting.
  • S-017 (WP17a, c5f181b): fix first; worst first. (1) Blocker: the surf reads as boats. From high and orbit every reef is ringed by hundreds of white, ink-gunwaled hull shapes (the breakers at rest past 170 u): a fleet of dinghies round each atoll in a naval game, and a sign of people (wp17a-ipad-orbit and iphone-orbit, every rim, plainest round the south-east atoll and the Great Lagoon's motu). From the deck the same surf is nearly absent: no breaking line on the reef front, only white paper triangles at the waterline (ipad-hero lower right) (coralAtolls.ts). (2) Blocker: the glaze cuts props at the waterline. Reef-flat pavement and beachrock slabs that cross the glaze sheet are dyed turquoise above a hard line and left tan below, so the reef flat reads as stacked hatched planks or rafts floating on the water (ipad-shore lower third, iphone-shore), and a slab on the dry spit is half teal (ipad-hero bottom centre). The glaze's 1.5 u cells show as faceted triangles with a straight turquoise-to-ultramarine step across the water (ipad-hero lower left at y≈860 of 1032; iphone-hero y≈540) (coralAtolls.ts). (3) Major: nothing reads as coral. The floor's coral heads are flat discs with concentric rings (sawn log ends, lily pads), some stretched into capsules, with blocky texels inside the green pads at deck distance (ipad-shore lower half; sea.ts floor cube); the near massive corals are dark slate low-poly lumps (coralAtolls.ts); flora's coral tufts sit on the surface as floating weed across the whole navigable lagoon (ipad-hero, iphone-hero), signalling shoals where a hull floats: false hazards (flora.ts). (4) Major: the channel is unreadable from the deck. Capture 23's stand-in shows no single channel: no surf framing the pass, no change of colour between pass and reef flat, three withies standing on dry sand at a spit; lace foam is a few flecks, so a captain cannot see where a keel would strike. From orbit the passes and the drowned reef sections are the same dark blue, told apart only by orange slivers and the white "boats" (coralAtolls.ts; the view in tests/pages/sea.ts). (5) Major: the atolls are racetracks. Pill-shaped rings of uniform-width bare ochre sand, no chain of motus broken by shallow passes, only 82 palms in the whole region (test log) so most rims are bare; the sand is ochre, not white; the reef fronts are orange wood-grain strata, floating as slivers round every rim from orbit and as a sawn-log stump at the hero's bottom centre: pastry crust, not reef (terrain.ts ground and walls; the WP3 reef strokes' shapes; coralAtolls.ts palm density). (6) Major: a toy-like near field. The nearest palm/pandanus is a dome of flat green and brown arrowhead triangles with no crown or trunk (iphone-hero right quarter, ipad-hero right edge); beach boulders and ramparts are pastel pink-tan low-poly facets, granite rather than bleached rubble (ipad-shore beach); the far rampart is a row of identical bread-loaf humps along the horizon (ipad-hero horizon) (coralAtolls.ts). The near field must be the richest drawing in the frame, not the crudest. (7) Major, shared: the noon sky is three flat torn-paper bands with a blank parchment strip filling ~40 % of the frame above the horizon, a camouflage mottle in the top band, identical cartoon cumulus, clouds lying flat on the sea over the Great Lagoon from orbit, and a grey hatched cloud sitting on the horizon behind the motu that reads as a rock (ipad-shore left) (sky.ts). (8) Minor: the glaze's drop-off is a uniform airbrushed outer glow round every reef from orbit; the withies' rose-madder pennants are a placed human mark (PROMPT Review: no sign of human settlement) in a team pigment (the rose madder slot); the hulk is in no shot; iPhone orbit puts the planet in the lower left under half a frame of blank parchment (coralAtolls.ts; the sea.ts mark view). Good: the Great Lagoon is the region's clear mark from orbit (a big turquoise ring with inked contour rings, a palm motu, a visible gap); the motu's grove from the shore (hatched trunks, drooping fronds, varied lean) is the best vegetation yet; the glaze's pigment range is right. Tests: nothing loosened (the diff only adds; the budget test covers the region unchanged at 420k/150 draws). But the clearance test asserts > 1 u where the code and log claim 1.6 u (CLEAR), only at centre-line points against part centres (a 1.3×-long breaker can overhang the pass), and never checks the channel's full width; its floors are slack (palms ≥ 50 vs 82, surf ≥ 300 vs 1066, stakes ≥ 8 vs 20): tighten them to near the actual counts. Next region: nothing at rest may look like a boat, no overlay sheet may cut props at the waterline, no concentric-ring discs, and the near field drawn best. — WP17a fixes (48ef503): (1) fixed: the breaker kits are gone; the sea draws the surf (sea.ts, rule SURF mirrored by surfAt for voices and tests): long broken spilling lines with green faces and lace behind near the eye, one broken lead-white ribbon along each reef edge from high and orbit; nothing at rest looks like a boat. From the deck the break still reads thin (a white line at the reef edge, wp17a-*-shore): open. 2026-10-24 — WP6 part B Tides, weather, the hour and the regions’ waters

The tide opens and shuts the Amber Shoals’ and Atolls’ passages on its 120 s rhythm, and untilTide tells captains when each opens or shuts. The seed’s weather — 48 spells of fog, squall and calm with fair between — drifts with the wind and forms by each region’s odds; squalls blow harder and in gusts (storm sails start to pay), calms slow every ship and flatten the sea, fog banks hide ships and trails from outside. The hour starts at dawn, afternoon or the blue hour and moves 3.5 h in ten minutes. The Ice Crown’s floes turn so lanes open and close; kelp slows and hides; the mangroves’ roots break the wind; the Maelstrom grips hulls and flings them out, at full speed under The Eye; hot water scalds hulls without copper. Checks: typecheck and build clean; npm test 101/101 (WP6: 24, ten new in part B), determinism vectors equal in Node, Chromium and WebKit; tick mean 65 µs, p99 271 µs in Node with ten ships under the weather (A9: ≤ 0.3 ms). A simulation package: no screenshots; the weather, tide, floes and vortex are drawn by WP13, WP14 and WP21.

2026-10-24 — WP6 part A Sailing, wind and currents

Built src/sim/tables.ts (forms, sails, helm, pilot, trim, wind, gusts, currents, fitting ids), environment.ts, sailing.ts, route.ts; the step sails all ten ships (captains idle until WP11); match format 2. Measured in tests/sim/sailing.test.ts and environment.test.ts (14 tests; full suite 87/87 green, determinism vectors and the browser replay included): caravel plain-sail equator lap 363.5, 359.2, 370.8, 367.9, 368.0 s over five seeds (the seed’s Great Current set aside; the coherent wind moves a lap about ±3 %); point of sail within ±15 %, averaging out over every heading, reference speed reached at 62.75° off the wind (pinnace), 56° (caravel, lateen), 65° (carrack), 67.75° (galleon); turning circles across reefed/plain/full: pinnace 7.1/10.1/14.1 u, caravel 9.2/13.1/18.3, carrack 12.7/18.1/25.3, galleon 16.2/23.1/32.2; 90 % of way from a standstill in 1.67, 2.60, 4.10, 5.97 s; the fleet rides the Great Current at 6.0 u/s; head home round the Weeping Isle: a 263 u route in 62.4 s, never touching land, the same voyage every time. The pilot lays off against cross currents and crosses streams it cannot stem square; routes home reckon currents as time (Sea.voyage). Not visible yet: no ship view (WP19) or controls (WP25).

2026-10-24 — WP5 App runtime, shell and test hooks

  • Boot to the title in 0.3 s after navigation on both devices, 0.9 s with the CPU 4× slower (budget 1.5 s and 5 s). The world (geography 0.9 s + nav 0.3 s) is built in a worker while the title letters itself in, 1.1 s in all, and read back from IndexedDB in 16 ms on the next launch; the cache key hashes the simulation's source, so a changed sim never meets a stale world.
  • Fixed-step loop: ticks paid from an accumulator, at most 6 a frame, interpolation alpha, a render clock that stops on pause and in the background (tested). Tests pace it with setSpeed (n ticks a frame) and setFrameRate.
  • Saving: the codec snapshot (and the recording, for a replay) goes to IndexedDB on the Capacitor App pause event (page visibility in a browser); on return the match continues from the same tick, and a relaunched app goes straight back into the match on the tick it stopped. @capacitor/app 8.1.2 added (CREDITS, iOS package synced).
  • Check 2, browser half (tests/app/replay.test.ts): 60 fps, 20 fps and 20 fps × 5 ticks a frame, each backgrounded mid-match and brought back by reload or in place, resume on the stopped tick and end on Node's 371ea12dc740b656. tests/app/shell.test.ts: title → harbour → match by touch on both devices, clean console, no DOM text, pause, startup.
  • Minimal screens on a cut parchment sheet, lettered with WP4's hand; WP26/WP27 replace them. index.html lost its SVG favicon. tests/sim/world.test.ts now times the build in CPU time: with the app's browser tests running beside it the wall time reached 3.8 s.

2026-10-24 — WP18 part A: the shipwright (forms, rig, sails, rigging)

One procedural shipwright (src/render/craft.ts, src/render/ships/forms.ts rig.ts shipwright.ts; API in WP18) builds the four forms on the legacy hull-form function (sheer, tumblehome, raked stem and sternpost, tuck and transom), with new castles, gun decks and sail plans: a low two-masted pinnace, a three-lateen caravel with no bowsprit, a carrack with a three-tier stern castle and a beaked forecastle, and a four-masted galleon with a beakhead. The hulls carry plank seams with staggered butts, tarred wales, the paint line in the accent, framed gunports with lids and guns run out (1/2/3/4 a side, tested to sit between the wales), channels, deadeyes and chainplates, tops, caps, crosstrees, stern lanterns, a capstan and a hatch. Sails are shaped on the GPU from the rig state, with the same function in TS for the tests. Topsail and topgallant feet are roached to clear the tops. Seams, reef bands and reef points are drawn in the cloth, and tears are cut by the paper's mottle and inked by the composite. The rigging is drawn as inked ribbons: never thinner than the pen, and lighter as the rope thins with distance.

Clipping test (tests/render/ships.test.ts): every rope and spar against every sail's cloth, over 4 forms × 3 canvases × 7 moods (flat, drawing, billowing, shivering at 3 instants, slack, half furled, nearly furled) × 14 winds on both tacks: green. Building the test forced these real fixes: stays collared above the yard below and under the roached foot of the sail above, the highest yards hoisted well below their mastheads, lifts made fast to the after side of the yardarms, braces led along the stays or above a lateen's sling, lateen runners set up to windward only, and square yards stopped by the lee shrouds (0.39–0.47 rad). npm run typecheck is clean. In npm test every WP18 test passes; the one failure is WP5's in-progress controls test in tests/app. Each ship is 3 draws; a hero ship is 24–40 k triangles (budgets and LODs: part B). The console is clean on both devices.

Style note for the WP2 owner: at ship scale the team washes read as opaque paint. A thinner glaze over visible fibre, stronger pooling at the edges and granulation in the canvas would carry the look of iron-gall ink and watercolour. Not changed here.

2026-10-24 — WP4 Lettering: the cancelleresca

  • The hand: 95 glyphs after Arrighi's La Operina written as pen strokes in the scribe's order and swept by a broad nib (38°, slant 8°), so thick and thin come from direction alone; pointed a-ovals, arches branching from the stem, clubbed ascenders, capitals with hairline lead-ins and swash hoods, joins from the baseline flick into the next stem, optical kerning, dots and t-bars added after the word. Ink: iron-gall thinning to brown between dips, pooled at stroke edges and ends, wicking along the parchment's fibres at display sizes, wet and dark at the pen's front while lettering in. API and decision (no atlas) in WP4.
  • Tests (tests/lettering/lettering.test.ts, 7, all green; suite 63/63): every character of every bold name in PROMPT.md and the captain's card line is in the hand; output bit-identical across runs and independent of other work; layout metrics (linear scale, joins, alignment, wrapping, anchors, writing order); no two letters closer than MIN_GAP (3844 pairs); exact coverage and stroke thickness w·sinθ + t·cosθ; at 11 CSS px ×2 and ×3 every stroke of every glyph peaks ≥ 0.6 coverage.
  • Cost: a 13 px label 0.4 ms (Node, M1 Pro); the title at iPad size 0.2 s and 8.0 MB (RGBA8, written once; rasterizeText is pure, so WP5/WP26 may move it to a worker); the specimen sheets hold 0.4–13 MB of rasters. Console clean.
  • Open: the capitals carry restrained swashes (the title's P and the chart's names use the swash hood and closing flourish); if review asks for grander display capitals, add swash forms per capital in glyphs.ts.

  • Legacy retired: 16 src/*.ts modules, the 44 scanned .jpg textures (18 MB) and their CREDITS rows, and the legacy tests (territory, tournament, determinism, capture, perf, shot) deleted; read them with git show 6ce29f2:src/<file>.ts. src/main.ts is a two-line boot (the one canvas over parchment). npm run check:assets: 0 offending files.

  • Premise confirmed: the engines' own Math.sin/cos/tan/exp/log/atan/asin differ in the last bits between Node 26 and Chromium 156, and WebKit 27.2 differs on all of them (and Math.pow). The d* functions are bit-identical everywhere: determinism vectors in Node 26.11.1, Chromium 156.0.8078.4 and WebKit 27.2 (minified production bundle) all give math 775364a3be0e0be1, rng 0672ccbfd59a20a3, sphere cf97d11f23205568, match 5e0fb7384f87a7d7 (a 2-minute recorded match; the same when resumed from a snapshot halfway).
  • Accuracy of the d* functions against V8's Math.* (fdlibm, ≤ 1 ulp), 200 000 seeded samples plus edge points each (tests/sim/math.test.ts); DoD bound 1e-12, asserted bound 4.5e-16–9e-16 absolute or 1e-15 relative:
    FunctionRangeWorst error
    dsin, dcos|x| ≤ 2π, and |x| ≤ TRIG_RANGE (2^20·π/2 ≈ 1.6e6)1.1e-16 abs
    dtan|x| ≤ 1e44.4e-16 rel
    datanall reals4.4e-16 abs
    datan2all (y, x)8.9e-16 abs
    dasin / dacos[−1, 1] incl. 1 − 1e-154.4e-16 / 8.9e-16 abs
    dexp[−745, 709.78]2.2e-16 rel
    dlog(0, ∞) incl. subnormals4.1e-16 rel
    Speed: dsin 4.7 ns against Math.sin 8.3 ns in a direct loop; datan 15 ns. The territory grid (512²/face) builds in ~20 ms; cell areas match an independent spherical-excess computation to 0.5 quanta (3e-8 u²), sum to 4πR² within 7e-5 u², largest/smallest 1.412.
  • Codec: a synthetic late-match state (ten owned caps, open trails, courses) snapshots to 26.8 KB (target ≤ 300 KB); encode 2.7 ms, decode 0.6 ms, hash 2.7 ms (moving the run-length scan into its own method took encode from 30.6 ms to 2.7 ms). Resuming from a snapshot at 12 random ticks of a 6000-tick recorded match always ends on the uninterrupted hash.
  • Purity lint on the TypeScript checker; a seeded fixture of 33 banned constructs is caught line by line, with no false positives on look-alikes.
  • Playwright WebKit installed (npx playwright install webkit) and listed in CREDITS. README rewritten for the new game.

Code audit of the earlier game (commit 6ce29f2)

The earlier game was a paper.io-style territory game (21 ships, cutting a trail sinks its owner, respawns, seeded random planet) rendered photoreally with scanned textures. Its engineering is generally careful and well commented. Its rules, world, controls and look are all wrong for PROMPT.md. Fates: adapt = move the named parts into the new module and rewrite the rest; rewrite = new code, legacy only as reference; delete. All legacy files are removed from the tree in WP1 and stay readable via git show 6ce29f2:<path>.

Architecture

Key numbers

QuantityValueWhy
Planet radius R200 u (1 u ≈ 7 m)Equator 1256.6 u. A caravel at plain sail in a fair wind (reference speed) sails 3.49 u/s → 6 min round the equator, as specified. Hard test in WP6.
Region spacing63.43° between adjacent icosahedron vertices ≈ 221 uRegion core radius ≈ 60–75 u leaves ≥ 70 u of open sea between regions. Rings at latitude ±26.565° (atan ½); upper ring at longitudes 0°, 72°…; lower ring offset 36°.
Sim tickTICK = 1/30 s; a 10-min match ≈ 18 000 ticksHeadroom on A14 and for the 200-match tournaments. Rendering interpolates; controls get immediate cosmetic feedback (stick, rudder, heel lead) so 33 ms tick granularity is not felt.
Territory grid512 cells per cube-face edge (1.57 M cells), equal-angle mapping; exact cell areas in integer quantaLargest cell diagonal ≈ 1.0 u; ships are ~4–7 u long. Proven in legacy check 1.
Ships per match10 (player + 9 captains); league 30 captains + playerPROMPT.md.
Devices (harness)iphone: 956×440 CSS @3, safe L/R 62, B 20 · ipad: 1376×1032 CSS @2, safe B 20Defined only in tests/harness.ts (WP0). Layout must also fit the smallest supported iPhone (iPhone 13 mini 812×375, safe L/R 50, B 21): WP25 adds it as a layout-only device.

A1. Layers and the one rule

src/sim/     deterministic simulation: all game rules, captains, world geography. No DOM, no three, no audio, no clock.
src/career/  deterministic career state and its pure update from a match result. Same purity rules as sim.
src/style/   the one style module: palette, pigments, line/hatch/wash/paper definitions, generated textures.
src/lettering/  cancelleresca glyphs, broad-nib renderer, layout.
src/render/  three.js world rendering; reads sim state + events, never writes them.
src/ui/      screens, HUD, touch controls (→ inputs), haptics.
src/audio/   synthesized sound and score; reads sim state + events.
src/app/     boot, fixed-step loop, save/resume, test hooks, world worker.

Render, UI and audio only read MatchState and the per-tick event list. The player and the captains change the match only through the same ShipInput struct. A purity test (WP1) fails the build if src/sim or src/career import anything outside themselves or use a banned construct.

A2. Determinism specification

  • Why: the iOS app runs in WKWebView (JavaScriptCore). JSC and V8 both give IEEE-754 round-to-nearest results for + − × ÷, Math.sqrt, Math.fround, integer/bit ops — and JS never fuses multiply-adds — but Math.sin/cos/tan/asin/acos/atan/atan2/exp/log/pow/cbrt/hypot/sinh/… are implementation-defined and differ between engines (and even between V8 versions and constant-folded vs runtime paths, notably **).
  • Allowed in sim/career: + − × ÷ %, comparisons, bit ops, Math.sqrt abs floor ceil round trunc sign min max imul clz32 fround, typed arrays, plain arrays and plain objects with fixed shapes.
  • Banned in sim/career (enforced by tests/sim/purity.test.ts): every transcendental Math.*, Math.random, the ** operator, Date, performance, timers, async/promises inside a step, Intl/toLocale*/localeCompare, for…in, Object.keys/values/entries on map-like objects, Map/Set in state, Array.prototype.sort without a total-order comparator (ties broken by id), string-formatted floats in hashes, any global (navigator, location, window), and imports of three/DOM/render/ui/audio.
  • Deterministic math (src/sim/math.ts): dsin dcos dtan (Cody–Waite reduction by π/2 with a two-constant split, then polynomials), datan datan2 dasin dacos (argument halving + series, as legacy), dexp (x = k·ln2 + r, polynomial, exact power-of-two scaling built from bits), dlog (exponent/mantissa split via DataView bits, atanh series), dpow only where unavoidable (prefer repeated multiplication). Target absolute error ≤ 1e-12 on game ranges; outputs are pure functions of input bits. Unit tests measure error against Math.* in Node. Built (WP1): Taylor kernels truncated below 1e-17 (sin/cos on |r| ≤ π/4, exp on |r| ≤ ln2/2), atan by two halvings and its series to t^21, log by the atanh series to s^19, 2^k from an exactly built table; worst measured error 1.1e-16 (sin, cos) to 8.9e-16 (atan2, acos), table in the WP1 log. dsin/dcos/dtan are fully accurate for |x| ≤ TRIG_RANGE = 2^20·π/2 ≈ 1.6e6 and deterministic beyond. No dpow: nothing needs it yet.
  • RNG: sfc32 seeded via splitmix32, state = four uint32 held in the match state. Named streams so systems do not perturb each other: world events, weather, loot, storm, and one per captain. Streams derived as hash32(matchSeed, streamId).
  • Order: every loop over ships/entities runs in index order; entity arrays are append-in-order with tombstones compacted deterministically; no iteration over hash maps.
  • Cross-engine proof: a determinism-vector test (fixed input set → hash of every d* output and of a 2-minute seeded match) runs in Node, Chromium and Playwright WebKit (JavaScriptCore — the closest proxy for WKWebView), and the iOS Simulator run in check 8 logs the same hashes. Any mismatch is a blocker.

A3. Match state, serialisation, hashing, inputs, replay

  • MatchState (src/sim/state.ts) is one plain-data object graph: header (format version, seed, tick, RNG streams), environment (wind phase, weather cells, tide phase, floe angles, storm phase/timers, caldera timer, hour), ships[10] (pose and previous pose, speed, turn, sail setting, hull, fire, list, fittings[6] {kind, tier}, consumables[3] {kind, count}, purse, bank, gull state, effect timers, trail cell list, home info, alive, sink order), territory owner and trailOwner (Uint8Array(6·512²)), entities (projectiles, coins, chests, finds, kegs, shells), captain minds (all AI memory as numbers), stats and the timeline log. No closures, class instances with hidden fields, Maps or Sets. WP1: the shape is declared once as codec schemas in state.ts (kinds f64, int, bool, bytes(n), words(n), list(kind, n?), rec(...fields)); the TypeScript type, the encoding and the hash all follow, so no field can be hashed but not saved. The skeleton holds seed, tick, the STREAM generators (world, weather, loot, storm, captains 1–9), the ten ships' controls (sail, course) and the owner/trail maps (0 nobody, else ship + 1). Each sim WP adds its fields and bumps MATCH_FORMAT.version.
  • Codec (src/sim/codec.ts): versioned little-endian binary writer/reader (Float64 for reals, varints for ints, RLE for the territory arrays). encode(state) → bytes; decode(bytes) → state; hashState(state) = 64-bit two-lane hash over encode(state). Because save/resume uses exactly the hashed bytes, the test "for random k: decode(encode(state at k)), run to the end → same final hash as uninterrupted" proves the snapshot is complete. Snapshot size target ≤ 300 KB. WP1: encodeMatch, decodeMatch, matchHash (16 hex digits); decode refuses foreign, outdated, truncated or corrupt bytes; a NaN or ∞ in a real throws on encode, and −0 in an int is stored as 0. A synthetic late-match state is 26.8 KB and encodes or hashes in under 3 ms.
  • ShipInput (src/sim/input.ts), one per ship per tick, integers only: steer int8 (−127…127), sail −1/0/+1 step, flags bitfield (breakOff, headHome held, callGull, useAimed, dive, …), consumable slot or −1, aim target as a territory cell index or −1 (auto-aim), chestPick 0–2 or −1, course (list of cell indices; set/cleared as an event). The UI quantises touch to this struct; captains emit the same struct from inside the step. WP1: ShipInput {steer, sail, flags, consumable, aim, chestPick, course} with course = cells, [] to drop it, or null for no change; FLAG {breakOff, headHome, callGull, useAimed, dive}; applyControls keeps the sail setting and the course on the ship.
  • Recording: the player's inputs as change events (tick, field, value), compact JSON; replay reconstructs the identical per-tick struct. Recordings are test data (in tests/), never game assets. WP1: {format: 1, events: [tick, field, value][]}; recorder()/record() (validating), parseRecording() (untrusted JSON, checked and copied), replay()/inputAt() (from any tick). The runner is in src/sim/match.ts: step(state, playerInput) and runMatch(state, recording, tick), which stops at a tick until WP8 gives the match an end.
  • Pause (chart open) = the loop stops stepping; the sim never sees wall time. Background → snapshot to IndexedDB on visibilitychange/Capacitor App pause; resume decodes and continues from that tick.
  • Results: the step emits a MatchResult when one ship remains: finish order, sea claimed, sinkings, treasure (share of bank × position multiplier), timeline (break-off, loops closed, trails lost, chests chosen, fights, sinking/win), and the observed player-style metrics the career needs.

A4. Career state model

  • CareerState (src/career/state.ts), plain JSON-able, integers only (fixed-point per-mille for learned weights): version, career seed, match index, player {rank points, tier reached (never lost), treasure, unlocked forms, selected form, gull {talons, wings, eye, beak, dive levels; plumage marks list (trait, level, tier pigment)}, style profile (break-off region histogram, loop-size mean, raid rate, run-hull threshold, fitting preferences, storm handling — each a decayed fixed-point average)}, captains[30] {rank points, tier, form, start-fitting tier, gull levels, learned counter-weights per habit with fade, record vs player (met, sunk by player, sank player), grudge flag}, career chart {seen-cell bitset on a coarse cube grid, discovered loot sites, regions named bitmask}, first-voyage flag, recent results for cards.
  • Pure functions: matchSetup(career, seed) → opponents (nine nearest rank; for the first voyage the nine lowest at gentlest), their forms/fittings/gulls/learned counters, player's form/gull; careerAfterMatch(career, result) → new career (rank points from position + sea + sinkings, treasure, unlocks, profile update with fade, captain learning for those who shared the match, grudges, chart discoveries); leagueRound(career) resolves captains' own results from Rng(hash(careerSeed, matchIndex)).
  • Hash: canonical binary encoding with a fixed field order (never JSON.stringify key order). Settings (sound, music, haptics, reduced motion) are stored separately and excluded from the career hash.
  • Saving: career after every match and the match snapshot on background, both in IndexedDB (src/app/save.ts); a corrupt or version-mismatched snapshot is discarded, never half-loaded.

A6. Shared files protocol

These files are touched by several packages. Edits are small and append-only, made near the end of the package, after re-reading the file: src/render/world.ts (register a render module), src/ui/shell.ts (register a screen), src/sim/match.ts (system order in step; created by WP1 — sim WPs run serially, so no conflicts), src/sim/state.ts + codec.ts (add state fields; bump the format version), src/sim/tables.ts (own section per WP), src/app/hooks.ts (add a hook), package.json scripts, CREDITS.md, progress.html.

A7. World data

  • Same world every match and career. regions.ts is a hand-authored table; geography.ts builds every field from it deterministically (d* math) so the sim (collision, depth, kelp, tide passages) and the renderer (meshes, textures) share exactly one geography.
  • Placement (proposal; WP3 may refine with reasons): north pole Ice Crown; upper ring (lat +26.57°) at lon 0/72/144/216/288: Fjords, Basalt Stacks, Kelp Shallows, Amber Shoals, Weeping Isle; lower ring (lat −26.57°) at lon 36/108/180/252/324: Glass Sea, Fire Archipelago, Mangrove Labyrinth, Coral Atolls, Spice Isles; south pole Maelstrom. Each region's Voronoi cell is one face of the dual dodecahedron (equal areas, which also makes check 4's "final calm per region" uniform by symmetry).
  • Per region (data): id, name, vertex index; local frame (north/east tangent); pigments (land, shallows, foliage, accent, ink tint); climate and flora species; landforms as strokes in region-local coordinates (bearing, distance in u; kinds: island body capsule, ridge, cliff headland, spit, bay carve, reef ring with channel gaps, stack/column field, arch, sandbar/tidal flat with height band, cone/caldera, ice-floe field, kelp bed polygon, mangrove channel network, hot-water zone, whirlpool); the landmark (kind, position, perk id, chart symbol); harbours (≥ 1: position, facing, moorings, repair zone); lighthouses (headland position, facing, beam period); fauna habitats (species, area, count); weather tendencies (fair/fog/squall/calm weights); loot richness and fixed find sites (wrecks, caches); mechanics parameters (floe rotation rate, tide period/passages, kelp slow and hide, hot-water damage, vortex strength, channel widths); music mode and motif.
  • Open sea data: fixed current lanes between region pairs (great-circle arcs with width and speed); Great Current route generator (seed picks a smooth path through several regions); open-sea find richness.
  • Generated fields on the cube-sphere (same equal-angle mapping as territory): height (Float32 6·512²), signed coast distance, floor type (sand, rock, coral, kelp, ice, mud, black sand), region id, nav grid (6·64², passable/cost, tide-dependent cells flagged). Dynamic geography (tide level, floe positions) is evaluated in the sim on top of the static fields.
  • WP3: the nav grid is 6·128² (2.5 u cells), not 64², so the narrowest designed channels (7–8 u) stay connected; each nav cell and edge carries the sea level above which it opens, so routes follow the tide. Built in 0.88 s (fields) + 0.26 s (nav) on the dev Mac.
  • Startup: generation runs in a Web Worker; a 128²/face level for the title, full 512² while the title and harbour show; cached in IndexedDB keyed by a hash of the region data (runtime cache, not a shipped file).

A8. Rendering approach (the illustrated style on a sphere)

  • Frame graph (WebGL2): (1) scene pass at dynamic resolution into two targets: colour (washes + hatching computed in materials, authored in display space — no HDR, no tone mapping, no bloom) and a normal/depth/object-class target; (2) one full-screen ink composite: silhouette and crease lines from depth/normal/class discontinuities (silhouettes 1.5–2.5 px swelling with nearness, creases ≤ 1 px, both fading with distance so the horizon stays delicate; line wobble from world-anchored noise), wash pooling (darkening near edges), the deckle (darker tone at the frame edge — the only screen-anchored effect), and per-hour grade from the palette; (3) UI in an orthographic pass. No MSAA; lines carry their own coverage. Half-float targets only (iOS lacks float32 colour buffers). Built (WP2): the scene pass writes an RGBA8 colour target (display sRGB; alpha is the veil that lets fog and smoke hide the ink beneath) and an RGBA8 aux target (octahedral view normal, ink id, ink weight) over a 24-bit depth texture; an edge pass turns silhouettes, folds, creases and material boundaries into line elements (darkness, half-width, boundary normal, centring offset) and the composite gathers them within 3 px into anti-aliased ink: 16 B/px, 91 MB of targets at the largest iPad at scale 1. Widths are in CSS px (silhouettes 1.4, creases 0.65), swell to 1.5× on the side turned from the light and thin to 0.4× on the lit side (lost and found edges), and thin and lighten over the view's fade distances. The per-hour look lives in the materials (paper, ink, shade and sun glazes per hour); the composite applies only the deckle and the storm grade. No 8-bit target needed more precision.
  • Paper anchored to the world: every material calls paper(p) from style/glsl.ts: fibre and grain from generated tileable textures sampled in the planet frame (sea, terrain), the object frame (ships, props), or by direction (sky). Grain octave chosen by log₂(camera distance) and cross-faded, so grain size is stable on screen yet never slides; foxing and stains are low-frequency and planet-anchored, so the chart and the globe share the same stains. Built (WP2): the octave is chosen per pixel from the anchor frame's footprint (so grazing views coarsen instead of aliasing) to keep a tile at 280 CSS px; proven by tests/style/gallery.test.ts (grain correlation 0.96 at the same world points after a camera turn, ≈ 0 at the same pixels).
  • Hatching: tonal art maps generated at startup (6 tones × stroke-preserving mips, 256²), sampled in surface-aligned coordinates (island-local triplanar with direction following the slope; ship planking UVs; cloud billboards), parallel hatching deepening to cross-hatching as N·L and occlusion fall. Shadow is drawn, never darkened. Decision (WP2): no tonal-art-map textures. Hatching is procedural: three stroke families (along the form, 70° across, diagonal) whose spacing nests by octaves — every second stroke of one octave is a stroke of the next, so strokes are preserved as they thin with distance, exactly the TAM property — with continuous tone as stroke width, per-stroke jitter, wobble, breaks and tapered ends keyed to each stroke's world position. Crisper at every distance than a 256² texture, no texture memory, no ghosting between tone levels. Strokes follow the form through hatch coordinates: cross-contours of an axis (terrain, rocks, towers, hulls) or the geometry's uv (spheres, sails, planking).
  • Watercolour: flat pigment washes mixed subtractively over paper; darker pooling at wash edges (from distance fields where available — coast, territory border — and the composite edge pass elsewhere); granulation (world-anchored noise) in deeper washes; soft bleed via noise-warped boundaries.
  • Pigments: only the illuminator's palette (iron-gall black, sepia, umber, ochre, verdigris, malachite, ultramarine, indigo, vermilion, rose madder, lead white, gold leaf) plus the captains' named pigments derived from them. Gold leaf is the only view-dependent shine.
  • Sea mesh: a camera-centred geodesic disc (polar grid, geometric radial spacing, reaching past the horizon) positioned on the sphere in the vertex shader — one draw call, no seams or pinching anywhere including the poles. Depth wash and coastline rings come from the geography textures; wave pen strokes are procedural in the fragment shader on a world-anchored jittered grid, oriented by the wind; foam from coast distance and the wake buffer. Territory washes are composited inside the sea shader (chunk owned by WP20).
  • Terrain: island patches built once from the height field (quadtree LOD from legacy terrain.ts), never crossing the chart net's cut edges (they lie in open sea by construction).
  • Deck → chart (one unbroken zoom). A single parameter z ∈ [0, 1] drives camera and morph. z 0→0.5: the camera lifts from chase to orbit; ships shrink to silhouettes, relief eases to chart relief. At orbit the world is drawn as the chart globe: twelve pentagonal sheets, one per region — the dodecahedron dual to the region icosahedron, so every region sits at the centre of its own sheet and every cut edge runs through open sea halfway between regions (≥ 40 u from any coast). Swapping the sea disc for the chart globe happens where both render identically, so there is no visible cut. z 0.5→1: each sheet flattens (sphere → gnomonic plane) and the sheets unfold about their hinges along a spanning tree (two "flowers" of 1 + 5 sheets joined at one edge), coming to rest flat on parchment like printed globe gores; per-sheet transforms are 12 uniforms. On the chart: a wind rose at each region with its 16-rhumb network (black winds, green half-winds, red quarter-winds), region names in calligraphy, coastlines in ink, territory washes, ships as inked silhouettes with heading arrows, the storm as a bruise of wet ink. In a match the net is laid out with the player's current region at the centre of a flower, so local courses never cross a cut; the career chart uses the fixed Ice-Crown/Maelstrom layout. Decision record: a single whole-world projection (Hammer/Aitoff) was rejected because the far-side regions (and, centred on a region, its antipodal region) would be smeared along the rim; the dodecahedral net keeps all twelve regions undistorted (gnomonic scale error ≤ ~1.25 at sheet corners) and literally unrolls. If the review judges the net unreadable as a portolan, revisit here.
  • Pause freeze: world animations read a render clock that stops while the chart is open (spray hangs, smoke stops, gull wings stay spread); only the zoom has its own clock.
  • Night: per-hour palette table (dawn, morning, midday, afternoon, golden hour, dusk, blue hour, night, pre-dawn) interpolated by hour; night swaps light hatching for silver-ink highlights, lights are painted glow washes, beams are hatched rotating cones. Lighting is computed in the player's local frame from the global match hour.

A10. Test & capture infrastructure

  • Harness (WP0, done): tests/harness.ts exports root, DEVICES (the only device definitions: iphone = iPhone 17 Pro Max 956×440 CSS @3, safe area top/right/bottom/left 0/62/20/62; ipad = iPad Pro 13" (M5) 1376×1032 @2, 0/0/25/0; each with its simulator device type), serve({production}) (Vite dev server, or a fresh build previewed from a temp dir), launch({headed}) → {browser, renderer, gpu} (Chromium on the real GPU, ANGLE over Metal, verified by the WebGL renderer string; SwiftShader fallback with a warning), webglRenderer(page), openGame(browser, url, {device, query, hook}) → Game (viewport, DPR, touch, landscape; safe areas emulated through CDP so env(safe-area-inset-*) works). Game: hook(fn, arg) and until(fn, arg, timeout) on window.__portolan, touch(fingers), gesture(paths, ms), tap, drag, pinch(center, fromSpan, toSpan, ms, angle) (real multi-touch), screenshot(path) at device resolution, throttleCPU(rate), problems()/assertClean() (console errors, warnings, stray requests), close(). tests/harness.test.ts checks the harness and needs the page to create a <canvas>. tests/ios.ts — npm run ios [-- --device ipad|iphone] [--shot out.png] [--wait s] [--no-build]: vite build, cap sync, xcodebuild, headless simctl boot, rotation to landscape (tests/sim-orientation.swift, a private framework, because a headless iPad boots portrait and letterboxes the landscape-only app), install, launch; exports buildApp, simulator, boot, rotate, install, launch (fails if the web view dies while starting), screenshot, backgroundAndResume, appProblems, terminate, pid; the web view console goes to $TMPDIR/portolan-ios/app.log. npm run check:assets (tests/check-assets.ts) is the file half of check 7. Toolchain: Node 26 runs .ts tests directly; Xcode 27.1; the simulators run on the newest iOS 27 runtime that runs their device type (iOS 27.0, as iOS 27.1 runs only the iPhone Duo), never an older one. Later WPs build on these and must not fork them.
  • Game hooks (window.__portolan, src/app/hooks.ts, built in WP5): getters ready (the world has arrived and the opening screen is up), screen, tick (−1 without a match), clock (render clock), paused, perf (startup worldMs worldCached frames frame tickMean tickMax ticks and the pipeline's stats); startMatch({seed, recording?, stopAt?}) (a replay holds still at stopAt, e.g. a capture tick), setSpeed(n | null) (exactly n ticks a frame), setFrameRate(fps | null), pinRenderScale(s | null), setPaused(bool), background() (resolves once saved; reload the page to relaunch the app, and the boot resumes the saved match by itself), resume() (back without a relaunch), matchHash(), show(screen) (any screen but the match; ends a match). To come: loadCareer/careerHash (WP10), events (WP8), camera(preset) (WP24). Hooks drive state for tests; they never alter rules.
  • Unit tests: node --test under tests/sim/, tests/career/, etc.; npm test runs every tests/**/*.test.ts (11 s after WP1) and stays under ~2 min. Browsers: npx playwright install chromium webkit.
  • Acceptance: tests/acceptance/checkN-*.ts; npm run check -- N; results to progress/checks/checkN.json and the checks table. Tournaments (checks 3, 4) use worker_threads over all cores. npm test runs whole in about 30 s (2026-10-24); its 20-minute runs were one test looping forever (a match over mid-lap makes step stand still), so no statistics needed moving: the 10 000-seed storm uniformity and 200-seed weather tendencies take seconds and stay in npm test, and the 300-match distribution is PORTOLAN_MATCHES=300 node --test tests/sim/storm.test.ts (check 4). A loop that steps a match must stop when it is over.
  • Captures (WP31): tests/captures/scenes.ts defines the 29 scenes (career fixture, region/hour/weather conditions, the moment, camera preset, HUD state). tests/captures/find.ts searches seeds and scripted inputs headless until the conditions hold and writes tests/captures/scenes.lock.json (seed, recording, tick). tests/captures/take.ts replays each in Chromium at both devices and writes progress/shots/<name>-<device>.png; the iOS variant writes progress/shots/ios/. Non-match screens (title, harbour, chest choice, results, career chart) come from career fixtures. The hour and weather always come from the seed — never from a debug override — so captures are real matches.
  • Determinism (check 2): run a recording headless in Node → match + career hashes; replay in Chromium with full rendering at 20 fps (and in WebKit), call background() mid-match, reload the page, resume(), finish → identical hashes.
  • Touch (check 5): only game.drag/tap/pinch — no hooks that change state — through first voyage, three matches, a gull upgrade and a form change, at both devices (time-accelerated with setSpeed).
  • Assets (check 7): scan src/, index.html and dist/ for image/model/font/audio extensions, data URIs and magic bytes; assert the DOM has no visible text nodes and the canvas is the only drawing surface.
  • iOS (check 8): on WP0's tests/ios.ts; the app reads a launch argument (via Capacitor) to run a scripted session (autoplay title → harbour → match → results) and logs hashes and capture cues; the host takes xcrun simctl io <udid> screenshot at each cue and compares iPad captures with Chromium's (stated SSIM threshold).

A11. Platform notes and risks

  • JSC vs V8 numerics — mitigated by A2 and the WebKit run in every determinism test.
  • Startup < 5 s on A14 — legacy took 6.6 s warm on a Mac. Mitigation: worker generation, staged LOD, IndexedDB cache, shader warm-up spread over title/harbour, lazy region dressing.
  • Tournament runtime — 200 matches × 18 000 ticks needs Node tick ≤ 0.3 ms and worker parallelism.
  • WKWebView audio — AudioContext must resume on the first touch; the ringer switch can mute WebAudio unless the audio session is set to playback (WP32 configures it natively in the Capacitor project).
  • Chart net readability — see the decision record in A8.
  • iOS Simulator WebKit (WP0) — resolved 2026-10-24. Under Xcode 26.2 with the iOS 26.3 runtime the simulator's WebKit crashed at page start about half the time on this macOS 27 host; on Xcode 27.1 with the iOS 27.0 runtime 26 launches (16 of them without any workaround) gave no WebContent crash, so the workarounds are gone. Still open: on this host the iOS 27.0 runtime's own daemons (intelligencetasksd, AppIntentsLiveEntityService, SettingsSearchReindexService) trap in an XPC entitlement check while a simulator is booted (about 20 reports in ~/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports over a 7-minute session); neither the app nor WebKit is involved. Simulator WebGL max texture size is 8192.
  • Context limits — region dressing and ships are large; each WP owns few files and reads legacy files selectively (git show 6ce29f2:src/ships.ts | sed -n …).

Work packages

Every requirement in PROMPT.md is assigned below; the status table maps sections to packages. Each package is sized for one fresh agent within ~150 k tokens of working context. "Parallel-safe with" lists packages whose files are disjoint and whose dependencies allow running at the same time (never more than two at once). Every DoD implicitly includes: typecheck and npm test green, console clean, evidence screenshots in progress/shots/wpNN-*.png, this page updated, commit of owned paths only.

WP0 — Toolchain: Capacitor iOS and Playwright harness (external)

Status: done (2026-10-24), by the toolchain agent — Capacitor iOS app runs headless in the Simulator with one command; Playwright harness on the real GPU with both devices, touch and safe areas; npm run check:assets. Commits a3d933d, bccdf15, 042aeb2, 655c5bf. Deps: none. Parallel-safe with: this audit.

2026-10-24 — moved to Xcode 27.1. simulator() picks the newest installed iOS runtime of Xcode's major version that runs the device type and never an older one: iOS 27.0 (24A434) for both the iPad Pro 13-inch (M5) and the iPhone 17 Pro Max, because the iOS 27.1 runtime (24A94425) runs only the iPhone Duo. New devices on iOS 27.0: iPad 89C9D0DC-0D90-4D6D-8D60-1753996F55ED, iPhone C5B68A28-F349-4EE0-B2AF-688C9ECDD51D (the iOS 26.3 ones are left in place). 16 launches without the workarounds and 10 with Malloc=1 gave no web view death and no WebContent crash report, so the Malloc=1 setting and the 5× launch retry are removed; launch() now fails if the web view dies. simctl screenshots now come upright in the device's orientation, so screenshot() no longer turns them. tests/sim-orientation.swift builds with Xcode 27's swiftc and still rotates (simctl still has no orientation option). backgroundAndResume resumes the same process and appProblems is empty; iPad and iPhone captures show the title upright in landscape, full screen.

Scope: Capacitor wrapper and ios/ project; one command builds the iOS app and runs it in the Simulator (npm run ios); reusable Playwright harness with device definitions, touch, safe areas, console/network cleanliness, CPU throttling. Owns: capacitor.config.ts, ios/, tests/harness.ts, tests/harness.test.ts, tests/ios.ts, their package.json entries. DoD: as set by its own brief; record the final API in A10.

WP1 — Sim kernel and legacy retirement

Status: done (2026-10-24) — deterministic kernel in src/sim, d* error ≤ 8.9e-16, determinism vectors bit-identical in Node/Chromium/WebKit, purity lint, legacy code and textures gone (log). Deps: WP0 committed. Parallel-safe with: WP2.

Scope:

  • Architecture: one deterministic simulation, fixed timestep, seeded RNG, no clock/frame rate, bit-identical in Node and every browser, headless — the kernel every sim WP builds on (A2, A3).
  • math.ts: d* functions incl. new dexp dlog dacos (+ dpow if needed), vectors; Rng with state stored in plain arrays and named streams.
  • sphere.ts: equal-angle cube-sphere grid, neighbours across faces, exact cell areas, great-circle helpers, horizon distance √(2Rh) (adapted from legacy sim.ts).
  • state.ts/codec.ts: MatchState skeleton (header, RNG streams, ships, territory arrays, entity lists), binary codec with RLE, 64-bit state hash; input.ts: ShipInput, recording/replay format; a headless runner runMatch(setup, recording) → {state, hash} stub that later WPs fill.
  • Purity test (banned constructs/imports, A2); cross-engine determinism-vector test in Node, Chromium and Playwright WebKit (install webkit via Playwright; record in CREDITS).
  • Generated, not imported: delete src/assets/, all legacy src/*.ts (record the commit to read them from), legacy tests that import them (tests/territory|tournament|determinism.test.ts, capture.ts, perf.ts, shot.ts unless WP0 adopted it); remove texture rows from CREDITS.md; rewrite README.md for the new game (run, test, layout); leave src/main.ts as a minimal boot that WP5 replaces.

Owns: src/sim/math.ts sphere.ts state.ts codec.ts input.ts match.ts (match.ts then shared, A6), tests/sim/math.test.ts sphere.test.ts codec.test.ts input.test.ts purity.test.ts determinism-vectors.test.ts vectors.ts, deletions above, README.md, CREDITS.md, package.json test scripts.

Built — the API later sim packages build on:

  • math.ts: dsin dcos dtan datan datan2 dasin dacos dexp dlog, PI HALF_PI TRIG_RANGE; vectors V3 v3 set dot length cross addScaled normalize (out-parameters, no allocation); Rng = sfc32 state as a Uint32Array(4): rngFrom(seed) rngU32 rngFloat rngRange rngInt rngChance; hash32(a, b), fmix32.
  • sphere.ts: R = 200, GRID = 512, AREA_Q = 2^24; cubeGrid(n) → CubeGrid (any power-of-two grid: nav, career chart) and TERRITORY; cellOf cellDir neighbor cellArea (integer quanta), faceUV faceDir gnomonicDir; fromLatLon localFrame bearingOf tangentAt tangentToward advance (moves a point and carries its heading, renormalised — ships never drift off the sphere), angleBetween surfaceDistance horizonDistance.
  • codec.ts: schema kinds, Value<K>, Format, encode decode hash64 — also for the career codec (WP10, magic of its own).
  • state.ts: TICK SHIPS STREAM SAIL, MatchState Ship MatchSetup {seed}, createMatch encodeMatch decodeMatch matchHash MATCH_FORMAT. input.ts and match.ts: see A3.
  • Tests to extend: tests/sim/vectors.ts (its match digest covers every system added to step), tests/sim/purity.test.ts (lints src/career once it exists).

DoD: d* max error ≤ 1e-12 on stated ranges (table in log); codec round-trip property test over random states; determinism vectors identical in Node/Chromium/WebKit; purity test catches a seeded violation; no image files anywhere in src/; npm start serves a blank parchment page without errors.

WP2 — Style module and ink render pipeline

Status: done (2026-10-24) — style module, ink pipeline and parchment kit; gallery at both devices and four hours; readability and paper-anchoring tests green (log). Deps: none. Parallel-safe with: WP1.

Scope:

  • The look › The rendering: paper (world-anchored grain, stains, foxing, deckle at frame edge), ink lines (silhouette swell, interior thin, distance fade, wobble), hatching (tonal art maps, parallel → cross-hatching following form), watercolour (flat washes, edge pooling, granulation, bleed), pigments (the illuminator's palette; gold leaf the only shine), one style module used by every material, effect and the interface.
  • Palette data: pigments in sRGB, the per-hour palette table (dawn rose/pale gold, midday ultramarine, golden hour amber/vermilion, blue hour violet, night indigo/silver/candlelight), the ten team pigment pairs and ten trail patterns (solid, dashed, dotted, double, dash-dot, long-dash, triple, chain, wave, zigzag) — Readability: an automated test asserts every pair of team colours stays distinct (ΔE thresholds) against parchment and sea at every hour palette and the storm grade, and patterns distinguish teams without colour.
  • Texture generation once at startup (paper fibre/stain tiles, TAM stack, granulation noise, brush/dry-brush masks), registered with sizes for the texture-memory budget.
  • render/pipeline.ts: WebGL2 renderer, MRT scene pass, ink composite pass, deckle, hour grade, dynamic resolution controller, counters for draw calls/triangles/texture bytes (A8, A9).
  • The interface's share of the style: src/ui/parchment.ts — torn parchment scraps, ink frames, button bases and the threat-vignette/deckle primitives every screen and the HUD draw with.
  • A style gallery test page (tests/pages/style.html, outside the game build) rendering primitive solids, a terrain-like surface and UI scraps at several hours and distances.

Owns: src/style/palette.ts textures.ts glsl.ts, src/ui/parchment.ts, src/render/pipeline.ts, tests/style/*.test.ts, tests/pages/style.*.

DoD: gallery captured at both devices and four hours (wp02-*.png) showing paper that stays fixed to surfaces while the camera moves (two-frame diff test), lines swelling/fading with distance, hatch tones, washes with pooling/granulation; readability test green; texture budget reported; no screen-space paper except the deckle.

Built (API for later WPs).

  • src/style/palette.ts (pure, Node-testable): PIGMENTS (the twelve, sRGB as they look on midday parchment at density 1), PARCHMENT, transmittance(c) and wash(ground, T, d) (the painter's model: a wash leaves ground·T^d in linear light; lead white, gold leaf and ink are body colours), HOURS and hourPalette(h) (paper, ink, shade glaze, sun glaze, silver, sky zenith/horizon, night; nine named hours blended in linear light), stormGrade(c, s), TEAMS (ten slots: primary, accent, pattern; no sea blues), watersColour/trailColour (the canonical way team colour is laid: TEAM_KEEP 0.75 of the pigment kept true at every hour, waters at WATERS_COVER 0.75, trails at TRAIL_DENSITY 1.5), TRAIL_PATTERNS + trailCoverage(p, s, t, aa), TRAIL_MIN_CSS = 7, SEA_WASHES/seaColour, NAMED_PIGMENTS (the captains' 28).
  • src/style/textures.ts: createStyleTextures() → tPaper 512² (fibre, granulation, mottle, bloom) and tMarks 256² (foxing, stains, dry brush); 1.75 MB with mips; ~50 ms on the CPU at startup.
  • src/style/glsl.ts: createStyle() → {textures, uniforms, palette, setHour(h)} (the pipeline owns one: pipeline.style; set uniforms.uSun from the light). STYLE_GLSL: paperAt(p, n, marks)/parchment(q), wash, granulation, washPool, shadeGlaze, sunGlaze, lightTone, hatching(c, dFdx(c), dFdy(c), tone, seed), hatchInk, goldLeaf, teamBody/watersColour/trailColour/trailPattern. inkMaterial(style, {vertexShader, fragmentShader, id, inkWeight, veil}): every scene material ends in writeInk(colour, worldNormal) or (veils: fog, smoke, washes over the sea) writeVeil(colour, alpha); built-in three materials must not be used in the scene. illustratedMaterial(style, {pigment, density, hatch: {mode: 'contour'|'uv', axis, scale, angle}, anchor: 'world'|'object', gold, marks, inkWeight}): the standard solid. Ink id 0 = sky (never inked); materials with different ids get a thin line where they meet.
  • src/render/pipeline.ts: new Pipeline(canvas); setSize(cssW, cssH, dpr); render(scene, camera, dt); ui/uiCamera (CSS px, origin top left); ink = {silhouette, crease, fadeStart, fadeEnd} (CSS px widths, world-unit fade distances: the camera rig sets them per view); storm; pinScale(1|null); registerTexture(name, bytes); stats (calls, triangles, render scale and size, texture and target bytes).
  • src/ui/parchment.ts: Scrap(style, {width, height, torn, frame: 'none'|'rule'|'double', age}), ButtonBase(style, radius) with pressed, disabled, sweep(amount, pigment), ThreatVignette(style) with setView(w, h) and set(angle, strength).
  • Gallery: tests/pages/style.html + style.ts (?hour=&view=deck|high|orbit&ui=0&dynamic=1, hook window.__style); node tests/pages/style.shots.ts [outDir] [--only=…] retakes every wp02 shot.

WP2b (2026-10-24) — true watercolour on parchment (log). The WP2 look read as opaque toon paint. Now glaze(ground, T, d, pool, q) in STYLE_GLSL is the style's wash and illustratedMaterial lays its pigment with it: transparent (it only multiplies onto the parchment, whose tone, fibres and stains show through), with an uneven load from the paper's mottle and bloom fields and occasional frilled backruns (all world-anchored), stronger granulation, and dark pigments capped at GLAZE_FLOOR = 0.2 of the ground's value so their hatching carries the shadow. washPool is a wider dried-edge ring (1.5 to 7–12 CSS px inside the silhouette); shadeGlaze is lighter (0.42, rim 0.3). PIGMENTS are re-authored as single glazes on vellum (umber, ochre, verdigris, malachite, ultramarine, indigo and rose madder paler; iron-gall, sepia, vermilion, lead white and gold unchanged); SEA_WASHES thinner (lagoon 0.35, shallows 0.6, shelf 0.6, deep 0.75); every hour's sky wash sits nearer the paper. Paper: fibre contrast 0.09, stains are five discrete water stains with frilled tide lines (was a tile-wide level set), foxing is 22 scattered spots plus four loose clusters, stain wash 0.4. The deckle is now a smooth umber tone with rounded corners and no texture of its own (the screen-space tear noise is gone), so every visible paper mark is the world's. Ink: silhouettes 1.6 CSS px, 0.5–1.5× from lit to shadow side; creases 0.7. API otherwise unchanged. Ship materials still lay wash() directly: WP18 part B item 5 should move hull and sail zones to glaze().

WP3 — World data and geography

Status: done (2026-10-24) — twelve hand-drawn regions built into one fixed geography in 0.88 s, tide-aware nav graph in 0.26 s, 14 world tests green, top-down dumps reviewed (log). Deps: WP1. Parallel-safe with: WP4.

Scope:

  • The planet: hand-built, the same every match; R = 200; open sea broad and deep between regions; every island, reef, channel, landmark, harbour and lighthouse placed by design in data; generators build fixed geometry fields from it.
  • The regions: all twelve at icosahedron vertices (placement in A7) with palette keys, landforms, wildlife habitats, mechanics parameters and landmark/perk ids exactly as the PROMPT.md table: Ice Crown floes/bergs/skerry; Fjords sheer cliffs and long narrow channels (chokepoints); Basalt Stacks pillar maze and the Arch; Kelp Shallows kelp beds and rocky islets; Amber Shoals tidal flats and sandbars with tide passages; Weeping Isle one great island (the largest enclosure); Coral Atolls ring lagoons with single channels and the Great Lagoon; Spice Isles dense crowded landfall; Fire Archipelago volcano, hot water zones, black sand; Mangrove Labyrinth twisting channels and the Root Gate; Glass Sea open water with no cover; Maelstrom whirlpool and spiral currents.
  • Lighthouses on headlands throughout; ≥ 1 harbour per region (natural, no settlements). Fixed current lanes; Great Current route generator; find sites (wrecks, caches) with regional richness (Spice richest finds, Fire most lucrative).
  • geography.ts: height, coast distance, floor type, region id, nav grid on the cube-sphere (adapting legacy world.ts SDF strokes); nav.ts coarse graph; generation fast enough for a worker (≤ 1 s desktop at 512²).

Owns: src/sim/world/regions.ts geography.ts nav.ts, tests/sim/world.test.ts, debug dump script tests/tools/geodump.ts.

Built — the API later packages use:

  • regions.ts: REGIONS (array index = region index in every field): id, name, lat/lon, climate, pigment names (keys of PIGMENTS), flora, shore profile, shelf discs, strokes in the region's chart (x east, y north, u: land carve bay reef with passes and tidal sills, bar column stacks flat cone kelp hot), landmark {name, perk, x, y, r}, harbours, lighthouses, cascades, finds, fauna habitats, weather odds, richness, rim + passes (Great Current lanes), mechanics (kelp slow/hide, hot-water damage, floe rings, pack slow/radius, vortex, eruption period), music mode and motif. TIDE {period 120 s, amp 0.6 u}, DRAFT 0.8, FLOOR codes.
  • geography.ts: buildGeography() → {heights, coast, floor, region, zones} per territory cell plus placed landmarks harbours lights cascades finds stacks floes lanes; ZONE bits (kelp, hot, tidal, lagoon, harbour, pack, vortex); CLEAR; CHARTS regionOf toWorld toChart chartTangent NEIGHBOURS; heightAt coastAt (bilinear), heightExact, navigable(geo, p, tide); greatCurrent(seed) → {regions, points (xyz every 2 u), length}.
  • nav.ts: NAV (128 per face), buildNav(geo) → {level, edge, cost}, navCellOf, findRoute(nav, from, tide, goal, toward, out) (A* or Dijkstra, ties by cell, deterministic).
  • tests/tools/geodump.ts: node tests/tools/geodump.ts [--sheet out.png] [--only id] writes the top-down charts.
  • Left to their owners: floe motion and collision, vortex flow, tide phase (WP6); perks, finds' payouts, eruptions (WP7); worker and cache (WP5); every look (WP15–WP17).

DoD: tests prove every region has its landmark, ≥ 1 harbour, ≥ 1 lighthouse on a headland, navigable connectivity (all regions reachable; atoll lagoon reachable only by its channel; fjord channels navigable at their narrowest), cut edges of the chart net (A8) ≥ 40 u from any land; per-region top-down ink dumps (wp03-<region>.png) reviewed for composition; timing logged.

Coral Atolls reshaped (2026-10-25, 8eedc17). reef strokes are drawn by their rim: rim knots (bearing, radius, flat width, crest) through a periodic Hermite curve, rimAt(rim, bearing) → {r, w, crest} (regions.ts; the renderer reads the same); geography.ts tables it per degree, roughens the rim line and flat width, scales the back-reef slope to the ring. New patch stroke (bommies, patch reefs: (x, y, r) triples at a crest, coral floor); motu() and spit() builders lay islets and sandy spits along a rim. New test: every atoll lagoon is entered only through its own passes, at both tides. Build ~70 ms slower (~900 ms here).

WP4 — Lettering: the cancelleresca

Status: done (2026-10-24) — the cancelleresca complete (95 glyphs + swash forms), crisp at 11 CSS px and beautiful at title size; specimen at both devices; 7 tests green (log). Deps: WP2. Parallel-safe with: WP3.

Scope: The interface › Lettering in full — a complete sixteenth-century Italian chancery hand (upper and lower case, numerals, punctuation incl. ’ “ ” – · : ; ! ? & and the wind initials), each glyph as pen strokes; broad-edged nib at a fixed angle (thick/thin from stroke direction), ink bleed into fibres; kerning and joins; a glyph atlas generated at startup at sizes from the title down to the smallest iPhone HUD numerals (crisp, legible); a stroke-by-stroke drawing animation API (for the title and region names lettering onto the chart); drawing APIs for WebGL quads and offline canvas art (cards, chart).

Owns: src/lettering/glyphs.ts nib.ts text.ts ink.ts, tests/lettering/*, tests/pages/lettering.*.

DoD: specimen sheet (all glyphs, pangrams, numerals, all 30 captain names, 12 region names) captured at both devices (wp04-*.png); smallest-size legibility shot at iPhone scale; animation frames of "Portolan" lettering itself; atlas memory reported.

Built (API for later WPs). Decision: no glyph atlas. Each distinct text is written once by the nib at the device's own resolution (joins and kerning across letters, exact pixels at every size, the writing time per pixel for the reveal) and cached; a 13 px label takes 0.4 ms.

  • src/lettering/glyphs.ts (pure): METRICS (x-height 10 units = 4.5 nib widths, ascender 19, capitals 15, figures 12, descender −9, nib 2.2, hairline 0.22, pen angle 38°, slant 8°, space 5.5), GLYPHS (a–z, A–Z, 0–9, .,:;!?'"’‘”“-–—−+×·/()%&, space): strokes in writing order, SVG-subset paths, roles entry/exit/late, taper, swash form; isLower. Built from shared moves (branching arch, pointed a-oval, clubbed ascender, capital stem with lead-in) so the hand stays one hand.
  • src/lettering/nib.ts (pure): flatten(ops, tol, maxStep), sweep(line, nib, widthAt, out) (one convex hull of the nib rectangle per segment), Raster (exact signed-area coverage after font-rs, union by clamped winding, no seams; paint keeps the earliest writing time and the ink load), blur.
  • src/lettering/text.ts (pure): layoutText(text, {size (em, CSS px; x-height 0.4 em), align, valign: baseline|top|middle|bottom, maxWidth, lineHeight, swash}) → {lines, box, strokes (CSS px, t0/t1 s, ink), duration, unit}: joins (minuscule exit → stem lead-in), optical kerning from ink profiles (MIN_GAP, letterGap(a, b)), dots and t-bars written after the word, ink thinning between dips; rasterizeText(layout, pixelRatio) → RGBA (coverage, bled coverage, writing time, ink load) with its CSS offset. Throws on a character outside the hand.
  • src/lettering/ink.ts: new Lettering(style, devicePixelRatio, onBytes) (pass (b) => pipeline.registerTexture('lettering', b)), .text(content, {…TextOptions, pigment: 'ink'|PigmentName (goldLeaf shines), progress, opacity}) → InkText (a ui-scene mesh; position = anchor; text, progress 0–1, opacity, duration s, layout, dispose()), setPixelRatio, bytes; cache shared between meshes, idle rasters kept to 6 MB. LETTERING_GLSL (letterInk, letterInkColour) for any material sampling a text raster (the chart's names). uiMaterial is now exported from src/ui/parchment.ts for it.
  • Specimen: tests/pages/lettering.html (?sheet=specimen|names|small|title|frames&progress=&animate=1&hour=, hook window.__lettering); node tests/pages/lettering.shots.ts [outDir] [--only=…] retakes every wp04 shot. Tests: tests/lettering/lettering.test.ts.

Built (API for later WPs).

  • Boot src/main.ts: canvas → Pipeline → Lettering → Game → hooks; then the world from src/app/worldWorker.ts (World {geo, nav}, WorldMessage {world, ms, cached}; cached in IndexedDB under __WORLD_KEY__, a hash of src/sim/** defined in vite.config.ts) and loadMatch(); game.arrive(world, saved) resumes a saved match straight into the match screen.
  • src/app/game.ts Game (the shell's Runtime): world, session, ready, perf {startup, worldMs, worldCached}, setSail(), startMatch(setup, recording, stopAt), show(name) (any screen but the match; ends a match), background() (suspend, saveMatch), foreground() (continue, clearMatch). Life cycle from @capacitor/app pause/resume (its web build maps page visibility). WP8: when the step reports the end, Game shows the results.
  • src/app/loop.ts Loop: run(stepper | null), paused (the chart: no ticks, render clock stops), suspended, speed (exactly n ticks a frame, or null for real time), frameRate, clock, perf {frames, frame, tickMean, tickMax, ticks}; Frame {dt, clock, alpha} (one object, refilled); MAX_CATCH_UP = 6 ticks, frames over 0.25 s are stalls.
