  • src/ui/shell.ts: ScreenName, Screen {ui, scene, camera, layout(view), frame(f), touch(t), dispose()} (built each time it comes up), ScreenContext {pipeline, lettering, runtime}, Touch {phase, id, x, y} from pointer events (fingers and mouse), the SCREENS registry (A6). src/ui/screens.ts: the minimal title (Portolan letters itself in; tap), harbour (set-sail button, disabled until the world arrives), match (the world view; no HUD yet) and results (“The voyage ends”), replaced by WP26, WP27 and WP25. src/ui/safeArea.ts: readView() → View {width, height, dpr, safe}, safeRect(view).
  • src/render/world.ts: WorldView(pipeline, world) with scene and camera; render packages append (ctx: RenderContext) => RenderModule to MODULES; RenderModule {frame(MatchFrame), dispose()}, MatchFrame = Frame + state.
  • Hooks: see A10. To come with their owners: loadCareer/careerHash (WP10), events (WP8), camera(preset) (WP24). The staged-LOD title world of A7 is not needed (the full world arrives in 1.1 s, 16 ms from the cache). iOS background and resume in the Simulator: WP32 (tests/ios.ts backgroundAndResume).

WP6 — Sailing, wind and environment

Status: done (2026-10-24) — part A: forms, sails, point of sail, wind and gusts, currents, collisions, head home and the drawn course for all ten ships, caravel lap 359–371 s; part B: tides, weather, the hour and the regions' moving waters; 24 tests; tick mean 65 µs (log). Deps: WP3, WP5. Parallel-safe with: WP13.

Scope:

  • Ship forms table (speed, turning, hull, guns per side, purse capacity) for pinnace/caravel/carrack/galleon; momentum and turning circle per form; sails reefed/plain/full (full faster with wider turns, reefed slower and tightest); caravel's lateen points closest to the wind.
  • Wind: one planetary field shifting slowly through each match with gusts; speed ±15 % with point of sail; exported apparent wind for sails/flags/smoke/foliage. Currents: fixed fast lanes and the Great Current carry ships. Tides on a visible rhythm opening/closing passages (Amber Shoals, Atolls). Weather from the seed: fog banks, rain squalls (stronger gusting wind, rougher sea, shorter sight), calms (wind drops, slower ships, flat sea), fair between; drift with the wind; regional tendencies (fog Fjords/Basalt, squalls open sea, calms Glass Sea/Amber Shoals). Time of day: start hour from the seed, sun advancing so ten minutes cover a visible stretch.
  • Regional movement mechanics: floes drift on slow rotation, lanes open/close, slow going among them; narrow fjord channels; kelp slows and hides (flag for visibility); mangrove tight channels; Maelstrom flings ships round the vortex; hot-water zones flagged for combat.
  • Collisions with land and ships (impact speed reported for reef/rock and ramming damage in WP8). Head home: shortest safe route back to own waters, predictable (exported for captains). Drawn course following until the stick is touched.
  • Hooks for modifiers: home-waters +10 %, Whale Road +20 %, rival slow in waters (Root Gate ×2), storm sails, spare canvas, lateen mizzen, chain shot, gust.

Owns: src/sim/environment.ts weather.ts sailing.ts route.ts, src/sim/tables.ts (forms section), tests/sim/sailing.test.ts environment.test.ts.

DoD: test: caravel plain sail, fair wind, equator lap 360 s ± 5 %; point-of-sail curve within ±15 %; turning circles ordered by form; tide passages open/close on schedule; weather tendencies statistically per region over 200 seeds; head-home route deterministic and safe; determinism vectors still green.

Built (part A) — the API part B, the captains and the renderer use.

  • tables.ts (WP6 sections): REFERENCE (2πR/360 u/s), FORM, FORMS {speed, radius, accel/drag/helm time constants, hull, guns, castle, purse, mass, half/beam capsule (WP18 hull lines), close/reach point-of-sail coefficients}, POINT, SAILS, HELM, LAND, BUMP, PILOT, TRIM, WIND, GUSTS, GREAT_CURRENT, LANE_TAPER; fitting ids SLOT SLOTS TIERS FITTING START_FITTINGS and SPARE_CANVAS COPPER LATEEN_MIZZEN by tier (WP9 adds the rest of the fittings’ rules).
  • state.ts (format 2): STREAM.wind (streams renumbered); Ship {form, pos, head (unit vectors), was, wasHead (interpolation), speed, rate (turn, +starboard: heel), shove, sail, course, leg, home, homeLeg, homing, torn (chain-shot seconds, set by WP8/WP9), fittings[6] {kind, tier}, bump, bumped (−1, a ship, or SHIPS = land), bumpOn (−1 stern … 1 bow)}; gusts[96]; MatchSetup.forms (default all pinnace); createMatch puts the fleet in two ranks abreast at the head of the Great Current under full sail (WP8 may move this to opening.ts).
  • environment.ts: World (moved here from the app), Sea and seaFor(world, seed) once per match (~45 ms: wind swirls, lane arcs, landmark cells marks, voyage = nav with currents as time costs); timeOf, windAt(sea, state, p, Wind) → {v u/s, strength (fair 1), steady, gust}, newWind, gustAt, stir (gust tick), currentAt, seaLevel(t), LOW_WATER, aground.
  • sailing.ts: sail(sea, state, i, input), collide(state), pointOfSail, settledSpeed, turningRadius, trim/Trim/newTrim, holds(sea, state, i, perk) (WP7 may move it to territory.ts), velocityOf, apparentWind (sails, pennants), BUMPED.
  • route.ts: helmOf (head home while held, else the drawn course until the stick is touched, else the stick through its response curve), planHome(sea, state, i, out) (quickest safe route to own waters over sea.voyage, open at low water; any ship’s route, so captains know the player’s; the ship sails ship.home). Until WP7 writes owner nobody has waters and head home holds the course.
  • match.ts: step(sea, state, input): stir → each ship applyControls + sail (captains’ input idle until WP11) → collide → tick; runMatch(sea, …). Session and Game build the sea with seaFor.

Built (part B).

  • weather.ts: vigour(spell), depthIn(spell, p); Sky/newSky/skyAt(state, p, out) {fog, squall, calm depths; wind, sight and sea-roughness factors}; inFog(state, p) (WP7: a trail laid there never shows on rival charts); veiled(geo, state, from, at) (WP8’s sight test: inside a fog bank the lookout is outside, or in hiding kelp the lookout is not in); hourOf(state); sunDir(hour, out) in a local east/north/up frame (rises east at 6, noon 60° up to the south).
  • environment.ts: brew(sea, state), the weather’s tick (spells rise over 15 s, drift downwind at 0.15 of the wind, fade; the next forms at a random place by its region’s odds, squalls ×3.5 on open sea ≥ 40 u from land); stir spawns gusts inside squalls in their prime; windAt scales strength by the sky and the mangroves’ lee (direction unsettled there too); tideOf, untilTide(level, t) (seconds until a passage at a nav level opens or shuts); aground includes the floes; floesAt(sea, t) (shared points; analytic, so no floe state), floeAt, offFloe; vortexAt(p, out) (clockwise from above, 7 u/s at the eye wall falling 40 % to the rim, inflow; added to the voyage costs, not to currentAt).
  • sailing.ts: kelp and pack ice in trim; scald(sea, state, i); the vortex’s grip (Ship.fling closes on its flow, τ 1.2 s; leaving keeps 50 %, all of it holding The Eye, then dies, τ 4 s; it counts in ramming speeds); floes stop ships like land and are slid off.
  • state.ts format 3: Ship.fling, weather[48] {kind (SPELL), at, v, r, age, life}, hour (the start; MatchSetup.hour pins it for captures). tables.ts: SPELL WEATHER DAY VORTEX. regions.ts: mechanics.shelter for the Mangrove Labyrinth.
  • Measured: fog 11.9 % of the time in the Fjords and the Stacks (≤ 8 % elsewhere), calms 17.8 % on the Glass Sea and 17.4 % on the Shoals (≤ 10.9 % elsewhere), squalls 6.5 % at sea against 4.7 % near land; squalls hold ~5× the gusts of open sea; all 36 radial lanes through the outer floe rings open and close in ten minutes; the vortex launches a ship at 2.2 u/s, 4.5 u/s holding The Eye.

Fix (2026-10-25): head home kept its route only while replans succeeded (seed 4 lost it at tick 721 and grounded). helmOf now keeps the route on a failed replan and replans an empty one on the cadence; planHome sets out from the open nav cell nearest a hull inside a coast’s clearance (PILOT.offing) and falls back to the present tide when low water finds no way; tryMove lets a hull part already on the shore move down it. Tests in sailing.test.ts: seed 4 held from tick 600, planning from a closed nav cell, replanning an empty route, a stern over the shore. WP7 — Territory, trails and doubloons

Status: done (2026-10-24), the claim-cost bound included: worst 2.4 ms CPU (θ = 70° N) against ≤ 3 ms, now a real test (see Remaining). Check 1 passes at θ 10–89° about both poles (part A, part B). Deps: WP6. Parallel-safe with: WP14.

Scope:

  • Territory: home waters; a small home disc inks itself where a ship slows after break-off; leaving waters lays an open trail; returning closes the loop — everything enclosed by trail and old border becomes its sea, including rivals' sea; purse banked. Breaking: a rival crossing an open trail breaks it — trail washes away, loop lost, unbanked purse spills as floating coins at the break, no hull damage; own-trail crossing does nothing (adapt legacy claim flood fill).
  • What home waters give: slow repair, +10 % speed, cheaper gull, steady income; rivals inside slowed and marked on the owner's chart. Harbours repair quickly (harbour speed).
  • Landmarks: enclosing a landmark grants its perk while it stays inside your waters — all twelve perks implemented here or as flags consumed by later WPs (Crown Light beam marks; Great Falls repair ×2; Arch reload −⅓; Kelp Cathedral hidden on rival charts in your waters; Sandbar Light chests ×2 faster; Weeping Isle income ×2; Great Lagoon harbour-speed repair and no rival fire inside; Spice Harbour gull half price; Caldera doubloon rain into your waters on its eruption rhythm; Root Gate rivals slowed ×2; Whale Road +20 % everywhere; the Eye launch at full speed leaving the vortex under your colour).
  • Leading: most sea flies the gold-leaf pennant (state flag); sinking it pays double. Storm swallowing removes ownership (API for WP8).
  • Doubloons: sailing distance on an open trail fills the purse (capacity per form); territory income straight to bank; plunder hooks; finds — wrecks, flotsam (scoop by sailing through), drifting bottles, island caches (hold position a few seconds) with regional richness.
  • First voyage rule: the player's first loop always succeeds.
  • Acceptance check 1 with a stated, justified tolerance (adapt legacy derivation).

Owns: src/sim/territory.ts economy.ts, tables.ts (economy section), tests/sim/territory.test.ts economy.test.ts, tests/acceptance/check1-territory.test.ts.

DoD: check 1 passes at several θ in both hemispheres with the tolerance argument written in the test header and recorded in checks; break/spill/bank/income/finds/perk tests; claim cost ≤ 3 ms worst case measured.

Built (part A) — the API the renderer, the captains, WP8/WP9 and part B use.

  • territory.ts: chart(state), the step's territory system after collide: each ship's move was→pos entered cell by cell (0.15 u samples, diagonal steps bridged, so trails are edge-connected and no crossing is missed); leaving its waters starts a trail (Ship.laid, map trail), a rival entering a trail cell breaks it (washes away, purse spilled at the break), its own trail does nothing, re-entering its waters closes the loop; then the pennant (state.leader, the holder keeps it through ties). Loop close: own sea ∪ trail is the barrier; regions race from the trail's neighbours (union-find; clean 16×16 blocks flooded whole, a word at a time, cells elsewhere) and all but the largest are claimed — rivals' sea, islands, self-crossing pockets, across faces and poles; rivals' trails inside stay open. inkHome(state, i) (WP8's opening: the disc where the ship slows), swallow(state, cells) (WP8's storm), atHome, repairRate (HOME.repair; WP8 mends), gullPrice (HOME.gull; WP9), intruders (bitmask; WP8's chart marks; the slow is sailing.ts trim), holds (moved from sailing.ts).
  • economy.ts: fill (ECONOMY.trail per u up to the form's purse), bankPurse, earn (income per u² straight to the bank), spill(state, at, amount, from) (coins spiralling out; WP8's sinkings use it too), scoop (any hull in reach: the bank, or an open trail's purse up to its room); coins sink after COINS.life.
  • state.ts format 4: Ship laid purse bank held (quanta); coins[64], leader, charm (MatchSetup.firstVoyage: the player's trail cannot break until its first loop closes), events (cleared each step; EVENT home, loop (u², the trail's cells), broken, spill, bank, scoop, swallow, lead), timeline (loop, broken, lead); emit, chronicle. tables.ts: HOME ECONOMY COINS. match.ts: chart → earn → scoop.
  • Tests: tests/sim/territory.test.ts (10), economy.test.ts (4), tests/acceptance/check1-territory.test.ts.

Built (part B).

  • territory.ts: holder(sea, state, perk) (−1 nobody), holds, perksOf (bitmask by landmark). repairRate(sea, state, i): HARBOUR.repair in any harbour's waters (ZONE.harbour; one in every region) and inside the Great Lagoon while held, HOME.repair in its own waters, ×2 holding the Great Falls. sheltered (Great Lagoon: WP8 lets no rival fire on it), gullPrice(sea, state, i, price) (home ×0.75, Spice Harbour ×0.5), reloadSpeed (Arch ×4/3; WP8/WP9), chestSpeed (Sandbar Light ×2; WP9), unseen (Kelp Cathedral: off rival charts in its own waters; WP8 sight), crownBeam(sea, state, out) (the beam's tangent, a turn per 12 s; the renderer draws it) and beamMarks (rivals within 70 u in the beam, bitmask; WP8 sight). Root Gate, Whale Road (trim) and the Eye (the vortex's keep) are WP6's, tested in sailing.test.ts and environment.test.ts. Claim: seeds join an adjoining region (a few regions per side, not two per trail cell), queues threaded through one NEXT array (merges in O(1), no allocation), empty blocks classed by an 8-byte pass, in-face neighbours by index.
  • economy.ts: earn(sea, state) (Weeping Isle ×2). forage(sea, state) (step: chart → earn → forage → scoop): flotsam and bottles drift on currentAt, never ashore; every 15 s a wreck there to take sheds 4 flotsam pieces; every 40 s a bottle comes afloat anywhere at sea; the Caldera erupts every 75 s (phase by seed) for 8 s, 16 coins worth 90 falling within 24 u of its shore, into its holder's waters (else lost) or anywhere while unheld; emptied sites refill; holding: the nearest full site within reach (wreck 4 u for 4 s, cache 9 u for 6 s) is Ship.prize, held Ship.holding s, reset on moving away; taken into the bank at value × region richness (wreck 40, cache 75), back after 180/300 s ±25 % (loot stream). siteValue, eruption (0…1 or −1; renderer), untilEruption (captains, UI). Flotsam and bottles scoop like coins.
  • state.ts format 5: AFLOAT (coin, flotsam, bottle) as Coin.kind (COINS.count 96); finds (each site's wait, s); Ship prize holding; EVENT find (by the site; timeline too), afloat (by the kind), erupt (ship the holder); scoop's by is the kind. tables.ts: HARBOUR, PERK, CALDERA, FINDS.
  • Tests: territory.test.ts +3 (harbours, Great Falls, Great Lagoon; Arch, Sandbar Light, Spice Harbour, Kelp Cathedral, perksOf; Crown Light), economy.test.ts +7 (Weeping Isle; every site reachable from water; richness; wreck and cache holds, refill, interruption; flotsam and bottles: rhythm, drift, scoop, life; the Caldera held and unheld).

Remaining. None. The claim bound is met (2026-10-24): a dirty block is flooded a row run at a time (the open run along a block row around a reached cell is labelled and queued as one node, SPAN its last cell), the same regions and whole-quanta areas, so claims and the hash vectors are bit-identical; this thread's CPU time, median of five, on a loaded machine: θ = 70° N 3.46 → 2.39 ms, 70° S 3.06 → 2.06, 50° N 3.34 → 2.02, 50° S 3.04 → 1.82, 89° 2.41 → 1.86; the cost test in check1-territory.test.ts is no longer todo. History: the claim cost, median of five on the dev Mac: 0.55–1.3 ms at θ = 10°, 1.3–1.8 at 30°, 2.8–3.0 at 50°, 2.9–3.1 at 70°, 2.5 at 89° (part A: 3.5–5); θ = 70° north measures 3.04–3.09 ms, so the cost test stays todo. The rest is the cell-by-cell flood of the trail's dirty 16×16 blocks (the cost follows the dirty-block count, highest where the circle crosses cube faces): next, row spans inside dirty blocks or 8×8 sub-blocks within them (plain 8×8 blocks measured worse at 70° and 89°). WP8 — Opening, combat, storm, sight and match flow

Rule change (WP12, 2026-10-24): the aft pieces (the castle's guns and the stern chasers) are light: they reload 3× slower than the same guns on a broadside (tables.ts AFT.reload, applied in combat.ts batteries), for players and captains alike. A ship that runs now harasses its pursuer rather than sinking it, and a broadside brought to bear is the killing blow (before it, the fleeing ship was the usual killer).

Status: parts A, B and C built, WP8's npm test green (2026-10-24); one open item below. Part B fix-up: the unexpected sinkings in tests/sim/match.test.ts were phantom rams — a ship riding the Great Current never cleared its blow fields, so at the throw-off the back rank "rammed" the front rank at a stale closing speed (up to 17 u/s); riders now clear them every tick and never touch each other (opening.ts ride, sailing.ts makesWay), and the three match tests pass with their scripts unchanged. Three tests predated the guns and were set right without weakening: the caravel's lap and the storm-alone matches strike the rest of the fleet's guns below (tests/sim/vectors.ts unarmed; armed idle fleets shoot each other, which ended the lap at 18 s), and the territory snapshot pins format 8. Whole npm test: 31 s, tests/sim all pass; the only failures are tests/app/replay and shell, from WP13's in-progress src/render/sea.ts (windAt throws on a point off the world). Open: check 4's full storm-alone run (PORTOLAN_MATCHES=300 node --test tests/sim/storm.test.ts) fails — with guns struck, idle captains still sink to rocks, rams in the crowded calm or hot water in about 1 match in 10 (heading home instead: 38 of 300), so the storm-alone distribution needs captains that keep clear (WP11) or a decision on what check 4 counts; the 6-match npm test version passes. Earlier list of failing tests, now passing: a ship that rides to the end of the current; a scripted match; when the player sinks, the rest of the match resolves at once, to the end it would have had. Deps: WP7. Parallel-safe with: WP15.

Part A API (2026-10-24). MatchSetup.fleet: ten Outfit {form, pigment, pattern, fittings, gull}; captains read passage(sea, ship.ride, out) (region now, next region, seconds to it and to the end); state.storm.origin is set from tick 0; parts B and C sink ships only through sink(state, i, by, cause); resolve() fast-forwards the match after the player sinks; result() returns the MatchResult; format 6. Tick cost over a whole match: mean 0.064 ms, p99 0.14 ms (A9: ≤ 0.3). Breaking off: the hull is held OPENING.peel off the current's local line until clear and the riders make way for it (collide), so it peels out into the region it is passing every time, its home inked about 3 s later. Open for part C: the 720 s deadline (END.deadline, end.ts) must be reconciled with the storm-driven end; it must not remain as dead or arbitrary logic. Done in part C: removed; the storm's last phase closes the final calm instead.

Part C API (2026-10-24). Storm (storm.ts): stormView(state, out) → {phase, calm, next, announced, toMove, toHold, rate} for the HUD dial and the renderer's wall and horizon (a Calm is {at, r, cos}, r angular); calmNow(state), calmAt(state, t, out), inStorm(state, p); the plan is state.storm.calms (stormPlan, state.ts: final calm uniform over the sphere, every calm inside the one before, centres on the great circle toward the origin's antipode); STORM in tables.ts (seven phases of move/sweep/calm/rate, announce 45 s, labour 0.25, sight 0.45, scan 30 ticks). Phases move at 90, 195, 300, 390, 465, 525 s to calms of 400, 260, 160, 90, 44, 15 u at 0.2–4 hull/s; the seventh closes the final calm to nothing by 600 s at 12 hull/s, so END.deadline and deadline() are gone. Hull: Ship.hull from fullHull(form, fittings) (FORMS hull, DOUBLED_PLANKING); combat.ts damage(state, i, amount, by, cause) is the entry point part B extends (repair is part B's). Sight (visibility.ts): survey(sea, state, out: Sight[], gulls?) once a tick gives each ship {ships, hulls, marks, range} (bitmasks; hulls: not hull-down) for captains (WP11) and the player's chart/HUD; trailSeen(sea, state, viewer, owner, k); eyeReach; SIGHT (eye/mast/hull heights by form, crowsNest by tier). WP9 hooks: Ship.flask/Ship.flare (seconds left: WP9 lights and burns them down), Eye {at, height, dive} for the gull. Format 7. Step order: …chart, noteFog, storm, earn…. Cost: step mean 0.089 ms, p99 0.19 ms over a whole match; step + survey mean 0.116 ms, p99 0.20 ms (A9 Node ≤ 0.3); survey alone 11.5 µs.

Part B API (2026-10-24). Combat (combat.ts) runs in the step after collide and a once-a-tick survey (match.ts SIGHT, there for WP11's captains too). damage(state, i, amount, by, cause) is the one way hull is lost: a ward (Ship.ward, lit and burnt by WP9) takes it all; the first hull a rival draws chronicles EVENT.engage (the timeline's fights); Ship.harm[by] remembers who hurt whom for the captains; a ship with none left sinks through sink. Blows from collide's report: rams (bow ≥ RAM.bow: hull per u/s of closing speed over RAM.speed × the bow's BOWS power by tier × 2m/(m+m′); the rammer takes RAM.recoil; a bow without a row rams as STEM_POST) and reefs/rocks (ROCKS). ignite(state, i, seconds, by) sets Ship.fire/fireBy (FIRE.rate hull/s; embers light any hull in hot water, copper or not); hot water through scald; mending by repairRate up to fullHull (WP9's pumps add there). Batteries (BATTERY port, starboard, castle; Ship.reload × reloadSpeed) fire on their own at the nearest rival in sight (Sight.ships) that is open to fire (targets(): sailing or peeling, not sheltered in the Great Lagoon) inside the arc and range, laid ahead by Ship.gunnery (Outfit.gunnery 0…1, default 1; WP11 sets it by rank). Guns per side by form, the carrack's castle (CASTLE) astern; balls spaced along the deck fire parallel, scattered by Gun.spread + GUNNERY.wild·(1 − gunnery), striking for damage by tier less falloff over the distance, × (1 + GUNNERY.rake·cos²) raking. GUNS rows by fitting (range, arc, reload, damage[tier], falloff, spread, speed, loft, blast, effect, lasting): WP9 adds rows; EFFECT.tear (Ship.torn) and EFFECT.burn (ignite) are handled. Shots: state.shots (Shot), launch(state, i, kind, from, to, gun, tier); shotAt(shot, age, out) and shotHeight(shot, age) for drawing; flat shot strikes the first hull in its path (swept capsule test each tick) or land, a blast shell bursts where it falls. Events: broadside (muzzle; amount the battery), hit (splinters), splash (amount 1 on land), ram, struck, ablaze. Aimed fittings: aim(sea, state, i, seen, cell, reach, speed, out) from ShipInput.aim (−1, a tap: the nearest rival in sight, led; a cell, a drag: drawn in to the reach) returns the rival, SHIPS for a point aimed by hand, or −1. Format 8. Tick cost with ten ships fighting: mean 0.13 ms, p99 0.18 ms (A9: ≤ 0.3).

Scope:

  • A match: ten ships, one life each, last afloat wins, ranking by sink order; fully determined by setup (career + seed) and inputs.
  • The Great Current: seeded route through several regions; all ships ride it at speed; swipe-up break-off peels the ship into the region it is passing; ships riding to the end are thrown off there; the current stays as a known fast highway; the storm's origin is revealed at the start.
  • Combat: hull points by form and fittings; damage from cannon, ramming (scaled with closing speed; rammer takes a little), fire over time, reefs/rocks struck at speed, Fire Archipelago hot water and embers, and the storm. Broadsides fire automatically when a rival is inside a side's arc and range; projectiles; aimed fittings resolve tap auto-aim and drag targets.
  • The storm: origin anywhere, phases closing toward a final calm anywhere (uniform over the sphere ⇒ uniform over regions); each phase announced before it moves, then sweeps and holds; damage rising each phase, sails labour, visibility drops; swallows territory; final calm small enough to force a fight.
  • Sight (visibility.ts): what each ship can see — curvature horizon by eye height and target height (crow's nest raises), weather (fog hides ships and trails from outside; trails laid in fog never show on rival charts; squalls shorten sight), kelp hiding, fog flask, Kelp Cathedral, marks (waters, Crown Light beam, flare, gull eye/dive). Used by captains and by the player's chart.
  • Sinking and the end: sunk ship spills purse and drops one fitting as a chest; bounty to the sinker (double for the leader); when the player sinks, the rest can resolve at once (fast-forward); MatchResult with finishing order, sea claimed, sinkings, treasure (share of bank × position multiplier) and the timeline of deciding moments.

Owns: src/sim/opening.ts combat.ts storm.ts visibility.ts match.ts, tables.ts (storm/combat sections), tests/sim/combat.test.ts storm.test.ts visibility.test.ts match.test.ts.

DoD: scripted-input matches run headless to completion with plausible timelines; storm phase geometry tests (nested calms, uniform final calm over 10 000 seeds); hull-down horizon test; snapshot/resume test at random ticks over full matches; per-tick cost measured.

WP9 — Fittings, consumables, chests and the gull (sim)

Status: done (2026-10-24): part A items, part B the gull in a match (src/sim/gull.ts). Deps: WP8. Parallel-safe with: WP16.

Part A API (2026-10-24). items(sea, state, inputs, sights) runs in the step after combat: timers burn (the patch kit mends), ShipInput.chestPick takes an offer from Ship.offer (a fitting swaps, the old one afloat astern in a chest its dropper cannot take back; a consumable stacks in Ship.consumables), ShipInput.consumable uses a slot (refused while one of its kind is at work), the stern works (the mortar on FLAG.useAimed through aim()/launch(); powder kegs dropped on their own at a pursuer into state.kegs, bursting under a rival or at fuse end), and chests afloat (state.chests {at, tier, fitting, from}) open under the first ship sailing within LOOT.reach with a clear deck. Part B seam: chestTier(sea, state, at, talons) (the glow and the gull's labour), deliver(state, i, tier) (opens on deck, or floats alongside while the deck is full), afloatChest(state, at, tier, fitting, -1) for a chest lost to the sea. Captains: worth, bestPick, canTake. WP10/11: MatchSetup.rank (first rank 1; pigments enter at LOOT.enters 1/2/3/5/7), startingFittings(rank) (OUTFIT); outfits are checked slot by slot (FITTING_SLOT). Interface/renderer: sternGlass, crowsNest, lookout(state, i, sight); events opened chosen swapped used keg kegBurst mortar shell, and chest for every chest afloat. Combat/sailing hooks: BATTERY.stern (stern chasers fire on their own; kegs and mortar reload there, × the Arch), GUNS rows with lasting by tier, MORTAR, KEGS, BOWS iron ram and cutwater (breakTrail, exported, holding while warded or charmed; Ship.cut), PUMPS, burst(), touches(); the gust (Ship.boost) and storm sails' gale drive (STORM_SAILS). Format 9. Tick cost, ten ships fighting with every item: mean 0.11 ms, p99 0.16–0.23 ms (A9 Node ≤ 0.3).

Part B API (2026-10-24). gullEyes(state) feeds survey and gulls(sea, state, inputs, sights) runs in the step after chart. Ship.gull (format 10): trait levels talons wings eye beak dive from Outfit.gull (each 0 … GULL.levels), flight (FLIGHT: mast, wheel, out, away, back, circle, dive), at, tier (−1 none; the glow and the labour from the call to the drop), prey, cool. FLAG.callGull pays callPrice() (bank, then purse; ×0.75 at home, ×½ holding Spice Harbour), draws the tier by chestTier(…, talons); the chest takes GULL.flight[wings] s (÷2 Sandbar Light): off along the bow, away over the horizon, back, once round, then deliver(), or afloatChest(…, −1) where its ship went down. FLAG.dive + aim (tap: the nearest rival via aim(); a cell: the rival within 10 u of it) halves the rival's way and marks it 4 s; cooldown 30/22/15 s. Beak banks 25/40/60 % of a purse spilled at a break. Eye scouts 20–50 u ahead, 6–15 u up (a lookout in survey); gullMarks(sea, state, i, finds, chests) lists the loot it marks. It wheels in danger (hull under 40 %, a rival in sight within 30 u, the storm within 15 u). Sight.gulls: rivals' gulls aloft in sight; bound(state, j): where one is going; perched(gull). Events called departed returning landed chestLost dive snatch spotted.

Scope:

  • Chests and loot: five pigment tiers (iron-gall, verdigris, ultramarine, vermilion, gold leaf), tier readable before landing (exported for glow and gull effort); opening offers three items, choose one; a fitting replaces its slot and the old one drops as a chest for anyone; consumables fill three slots, stacking by kind. Loot tables by tier, rank (higher tiers enter as rank rises) and region richness.
  • Fittings (six slots on every form; all five tiers scale strength): Bow — stem post, iron ram, cutwater (breaks target's open trail; target cannot lay a new one for a few seconds). Broadside — light, long, swivel guns, chain shot (tears sails, slows), heated shot (fires). Stern — stern chasers, powder kegs, mortar (lobs over islands at a chosen point). Rig — spare canvas, lateen mizzen, storm sails. Hull — copper sheathing (faster, immune to hot water), doubled planking, pumps. Lookout — stern glass, crow's nest, lookout (flags for render/UI).
  • Consumables: patch kit (hull over 3 s), ward (absorbs damage; trail unbreakable for a few seconds), gust, fog flask (hidden from rival charts and lookouts), flare (reveal within a wide radius to you alone).
  • The gull in a match: call by spending doubloons (price; home-waters discount; Spice Harbour half); flies over the horizon and returns with a chest in seconds (Wings; Sandbar Light ×2), circles and drops it on deck; rivals that see the gull see its destination; a ship that sinks before the chest lands loses it to the sea for anyone. Traits from career: Talons (max tier), Wings, Eye (scouts and marks loot and ships beyond the horizon), Beak (snatch back a share of spilled purse), Dive (reveal + slow nearest rival, cooldown by level).

Owns: src/sim/items.ts gull.ts, tables.ts (fittings/consumables/tiers sections), tests/sim/items.test.ts gull.test.ts.

DoD: every fitting and consumable has a behaviour test at tiers 1 and 5; gull delivery/loss/Beak/Dive tests; loot pool gating by rank; landmark perks that touch items honoured (Arch, Sandbar Light, Spice Harbour, Great Lagoon).

Part A API (2026-10-24). src/career/state.ts: Career (codec CAREER_FORMAT 'PTLC' v1, integers only: seed, match, player {points, tier, treasure, form, gull {talons wings eye beak dive}}, captains[30] {points}), newCareer(seed) (captains shuffled over the rose 10…779), encodeCareer/decodeCareer/careerHash/copyCareer, RANK, tierOf, unlockedForms, rankDeltas(Outcome[]). league.ts: CAPTAINS (the table; league index = Outfit.pigment = EMBLEMS index, the player LEAGUE = 30, the gull), TEMPERAMENTS, TRAITS, draw(career) (nine nearest by points, ties to the lower index), captainForm (preferred once unlocked, else the greatest unlocked), captainGull(tier, temperament) (a level per two tiers, two temperament traits a level ahead), captainOutfit (form, pigment, pattern 1 + temperament, startingFittings(tier), gull, GUNNERY by tier), leagueRound (the 21 who did not sail race heats of 7 table neighbours: edge 0.05 per rank point over the heat mean, temperament luck/poise/sea/hunt, scored by the same formula). setup.ts: toSetup(career, seed) → {setup (rank = tier, firstVoyage = match 0, fleet of ten), drawn}. progress.ts: applyResult(career, MatchResult) (player by the formula floored at its tier, treasure home, the nine drawn by the real result, league round, match + 1), chooseForm, GULL_PRICES (Talons 150/400/900/1800, Wings and Eye 100/250/550/1100, Beak 150/450/1000, Dive 200/550/1200), gullPrice, buyGull, plumage(gull) → {marks {trait, level, pigment tier: iron-gall … vermilion at a trait's top}, goldTips}. App: save.ts saveCareer/loadCareer (store 'career', DB v2; an unreadable career is deleted, a new one begins); game.ts set sail fits out toSetup(career, seed), a voyage without a recording applies its result when its match ends and saves; main.ts loads the career at boot.

Rank termPoints (n ships, place p)Intent
Place20·(n + 1 − 2p)/(n − 1): winner +20 … first to sink −20outlasting the fleet is the heart of a match; every ship outlasted is worth the same
Sea8·(n·claimed − Σ)/Σ, within −8 … +16claiming sea pays even for a ship that sinks early; twice the match's mean +8
Sinkings+5 per ship sunk, −5 if a ship sank itfighting pays, as points taken from the sunk
Floorthe player's points never below (tier − 1)·100; all within 0 … 799a tier once reached is never lost; the terms sum to nothing, so the league neither swells nor drains

Part B API (2026-10-24). src/career/voyage.ts: newVoyage(); observe(voyage, sea, state) after every step, reading only: the player's events each tick, and every 15 ticks its sight (survey), inking the chart grid CHART_GRID (cube 64², about 5 u a cell) out to min(air sight, lookout horizon + 20 u), finds in reach, the region it is in, runs (3 samples sailing away from the rival that hit it within 20 s: hull %), its margin inside the calm while the edge is within 60 u; habitsOf; lessons by LESSON (break-off, steer, loop, gull call, chest choice, broadside, storm run: first tick each); toLetter(career, voyage) regions to letter and when; chartInked(career) permille per region. applyResult(career, result, voyage?) calls learn.ts remember: each drawn captain's record (shared, sank, sunk) and grudge 0…3 (one more per sinking by the player, spent by a match together without one) from the result; with the voyage the profile and the nine learn, the other 21 fade, and the chart inks (seen cells, found sites, region named {match, tick}). Rates (state.ts LEARNING): each match together moves every habit weight 40 % of the way toward what the match showed; a match apart or showing nothing fades it 15 %; a captain knows a habit at 500‰, so a steady habit is known after 2 matches together and forgotten 2 together or 4 apart after the player drops it. Bins (BINS): loop radius 10/20/35 u, raids 1/3, run hull 30/60 % and stands-and-fights, storm margin 0/20 u. toSetup adds setup.minds (fleet order, the player null; Mind in sim/state.ts: gentle, grudge, breakOff/loop/raids/run/storm as {value, confidence‰}, fittings) and setup.known (find sites charted). First voyage (match 0): the nine lowest, starting kit, first-rank gull, gunnery 0.2 (GENTLE), gentle minds, firstVoyage → charm. Cards: cardLine/cardLines/recordLine, texts in CARD (every character in the hand's glyphs). Career format v2: a v1 save is refused and cleared by loadCareer. Remaining (others): the app keeps a voyage beside each match and passes it to applyResult (WP26/WP27); WP11/WP12 carry setup.minds into the captains and act on them; WP27 letters lessons and region names and draws the chart (coastlines at CHART.sketch % on unseen parchment). WP11 needs each ship's captain (temperament, tier) inside the sim: an Outfit field that setup.ts fills.

Scope: Rank (eight winds Greco → Tramontana, 100 points each, tiers never lost; unlocks pigment tiers, and forms at Levante/Ostro/Ponente); rank points from position, sea claimed and ships sunk; treasure; the league (exactly the thirty captains of the PROMPT.md table with meaning, temperament, form, pigments, emblem); draw the nine nearest the player's rank; between matches resolve captains' results against one another from the career state; higher-ranked captains start with better fittings and stronger gulls; captains choose forms by temperament; gull upgrades bought with treasure (Talons 4, Wings 4, Eye 4, Beak 3, Dive 3 levels) and the plumage record (marks per level in the tier pigment; gold-leaf wingtips at the top of every trait); the player profile (break-off place, loop size, raid rate, hull at which it runs, favoured fittings, storm handling) and each captain's learning from shared matches, fading over matches; memory of being sunk (record vs player, harder hunting next time); card one-liners ("Has learned you break off at the volcano."); the career chart data (inks what was seen: coastlines, reefs, currents, lighthouses, harbours, landmarks, wrecks, caches; loot sites remembered for later matches; region named on first entry); first voyage setup (nine lowest captains at their gentlest); saving after every match; career codec and hash (A4).

Storm margin (stormMargin): at most BRAIN.inside 0.4 of the calm’s radius, so in the 15 u final calm a captain holds ground 9 u out rather than the centre. Diagnostics (200 matches): rocks sank 0.95 captains a match before minute 8, 0.43 of them mending at harbours before the turning-circle lookout (0.74 and 0.29 after); 1012 ship-s a match below half hull, 460 of them with a harbour within 120 u and no rival within 40 u. Tried and reverted: (i) cornered: from the final calm’s announcement no captain flees, makes for home or mends, and its will to fight rises by 1 or 2: the storm sank the last to fall in 145 (from 155), will 1 and 2 alike; (ii) with it, inside the last calm before the closing phase it fights on rather than steering for the centre: 147; (iii) charting a course round land to the calm through the nav graph (findRoute) when land lies across a storm-mode ship’s way: 126, but 195 of 200 in 9–11 min (min 8.26) and hunters’ lead t 1.09, tick 0.18 ms. Finding: at 580–600 s storm-mode ships make a median 2 u toward the calm in 20 s (mean speed 1.5 u/s); 231 of 613 are over 60 u out and stuck behind land: that, not the margin, is why the storm finishes the finale. More ships reaching the late calms makes the squeeze kill sooner, so (iii) needs check 4’s attrition cut further first. Tools: tournament.ts mode arrays hold 16 modes. Tests: sim/career 196/196 in Node; determinism vectors bit-identical in Node, Chromium and WebKit.

Remaining: (1) Check 3 does not yet pass: its large-loops clause (S-018 a, S-023 3) is not met and not asserted, left to WP12 part F. Hunters beyond the player's border were not counted in part D: hunters beyond its border, learn − naive, by match −0.13 0.03 −0.05 −0.04 −0.23 0.01 −0.14 −0.06 −0.01 −0.06; known loops 0–2.8 captains. Next as in part C: a hunter that knows wide loops patrols from the player’s known region, not only within BRAIN.stalk of its own anchor; then a stated margin on both halves beside the region clause. (2) A fought finale: the storm sinks the last to fall in 163 of 200. Cause: storm-mode ships stuck behind land (part D finding). Chart a course round it (part D (iii)) once the late squeeze no longer ends matches early; then (i)/(ii). (3) Check 4’s margin is thin (min 9.13): the idle player dying to the storm can end a match (27 at 8.26 under (iii)), and one hunter can sink six (197). (4) S-018 (6): hunters are still told by stalk ≥ 0.8; KITS has no unit test. (5) Part E (S-023 1, 2, 4, 5) is unfinished and not on main. It is saved on branch wp12e-wip (6f70299). It holds: no mending with prey it can finish or an intruder in sight (holdsOn); harbours and storm courses charted round land and unstemmable currents (chart, findRoute with a cost limit); a current crab; mark-capped hazard probes; errands bounded by the calm; reefing in small calms and when coming about; no running, banking or mending in calms under 50 u. On 60 matches (node tests/tools/tournament.ts --matches 60 --workers 6) the storm sank the last to fall in 16 (52 at HEAD), with lengths 9.27–10.79 min. But turtles now win 47% of matches, take the finale kills (sunk 1.31 against hunters 1.41) and hold hunters’ lead to t 0.44, so check 3 would fail. Findings: in the 15 u final calm, full-sail carracks and galleons (turning radius 12.6 and 16.1 u) overshoot their mark and circle out; captains leave the calm for chests and rams; lane currents head-on at 2.7 u/s stall storm-mode ships whose straight course looks clear. Not measured: the look-ahead trim flip (S-023 5) and rock sinkings while mending.

Scope: captains learn the player in play (huge loops ⇒ hunters patrol beyond its border; favourite break-off ⇒ more captains break off there; runs when wounded ⇒ they press when it is wounded); grudge behaviour; the 200-match tournament runner (worker_threads); check 3 (distinct temperament profiles over sea claimed, trails broken, ships sunk, loot taken, finishing position; hunters lead sinkings; a stated rank margin; scripted player countered within five matches and the counter fades within five after it changes habits); check 4 (9–11 min matches; every region chosen as a break-off by someone; final calm per region roughly equal with a stated chi-square bound); tune economy, storm timing and captain parameters until both pass with margin — every match has calm, tension and payoff.

WP13 — The sea

Status: done (2026-10-24) — part A (disc, swell, depth glazes, coast rings, strokes, foam, hull-down; log) and part B (floors, weather, wakes, territory and current hooks, richness; log); one follow-up, the game sea's wind, under Remaining. Deps: WP2, WP3, WP5. Parallel-safe with: WP6.

Scope: The look › The sea — bare parchment washed with depth (deep indigo → ultramarine shelves → verdigris shallows → pale lagoons); coastlines traced by rings of fine parallel lines spreading from shore; waves as small animated pen strokes riding the swell in the wind direction; lead-white dry-brush foam at coasts, reefs, bows and wakes; shallows showing floors in hatched rock, coral, sand ripples and swaying kelp. Seamless camera-centred sea mesh (A8); render swell (great-circle Gerstner from legacy, TS + GLSL) for ships and floating things; calm-mirror and squall-rough states from sim weather; the planet's curve always visible with hull-down sinking of distant objects (masthead last) and delicate far horizon; territory chunk hook for WP20; wake buffer.

Owns: src/render/sea.ts swell.ts, tests/render/sea.*.

DoD: shots at deck and high camera, four hours, calm and squall, a coast with rings and foam, shallows over each floor type (wp13-*.png); hull-down test shot; no seams at cube edges or poles (pole shot); draw calls/triangles reported.

Built (part A, API for later WPs). src/render/swell.ts: SWELL_GLSL — seaField(dir) (height, coast distance, lagoon, reef from one RGBA16F cube texture, 12.6 MB, whose texels sit on the sim's equal-angle cells with the lookup warped to match, so filtering is seamless across cube edges), swellAmp(depth), swell(dir, t, amp, out normal, out lift) (16 great-circle Gerstner waves, 4–32 u, weighted by the wind, sheltered in shallow water) — and Swell, its TypeScript twin: uniforms (spread into any material that rides the sea: uSwellAmp master amplitude for calm/squall, uWindAxis/uWind, uSeaLevel the tide, tSeaField), setWind(p, toward, strength), displace(rest, t, out) (the mesh vertex, exactly), sample(p, t, out) → height and normal over p, ride(p, forward, length, beam, t, out) → position and deck up; seaFieldFaces(geo). src/render/sea.ts: Sea(pipeline, geo) {mesh, swell, setWind, triangles}, discShape(height), createSea registered in MODULES (it also sets uTime from the frame's render clock). The disc: 112 rings × 224 segments (50,176 triangles, one draw call) laid round the eye's foot with a parallel-transported frame (no spin at a pole), reaching 1.2× the horizon arc + 6 u, its first ring 3 % of the eye height out. The fragment: four depth glazes from SEA_WASHES at a reduced load (lagoon 0.5, shallows 0.45, shelf 0.32, deep 0.38 of their density) with wavering pooled edges at 3, 16 and 24 u; the swell's backs take one more glaze with a frilled, pooled edge (brushed bands that travel with the swell); six coast rings from the coast-distance field spreading by 1.5×, with a nib tremor, fading outward and wherever they would pack tighter than 5 px; the waterline in ink; lead-white dry brush along the waterline, on bores running in every 6.5 s and on reef crests as the swell lifts; wave strokes (crescents along the crest, bowed downwind, drawn on, drifting, lifting off; two octaves on the dominant planet-frame plane) favouring crests and thinning toward the horizon; the far sea pales. Test page tests/pages/sea.html (free camera: drag, wheel, WASD; views deck, high, coast, hull, seam, pole, curve); node tests/pages/sea.shots.ts retakes the wp13 shots. Cost: the sea is 1 draw call and 50 k triangles at every view (the deck frame with three ships: 13 calls, 139 k triangles); the field cube takes 30–36 ms to build at match open. tests/render/sea.test.ts: field layout against the GL cube-map rule, TS/GPU swell agreement and riding, continuity over a cube edge and the pole, calm over land, the disc past the horizon at every height.

Built (part B, API for later WPs). src/render/sea.ts: the floor seen through clear shallows — sand ripples parallel to the shore, hatched and cracked boulders, coral heads ringed, banded and stippled, kelp-forest shade with swaying blades — refracted and wavering under the swell, fading with depth (clearest over lagoons and kelp), from an RGBA8 floor cube beside tSeaField (seaFloorFaces, 6.3 MB; cubeTexture/GLSL cubeAt in swell.ts read any cube on the TERRITORY cells). Weather: Sea.setWeather({rough, calm, squall}, dt) (the sim's skyAt, eased over ~2.5 s) drives uSwellAmp and the stroke and whitecap shares; a calm lays a mirror (the sky's wash, islands found by marching the reflected ray over the height field, ships from the wake slots, drawn upside down and broken by ripple strokes); a squall greys and roughens the sea with lead-white whitecaps and spindrift streaks. createSea feeds skyAt and tideOf at the eye's foot every frame, so the tide moves the waterline on the Amber Shoals and Atolls. Wake hook (WP19): per ship per frame seas.get(scene).wakes.feed(id, pos, forward, speed, f.clock, length, beam), and wakes.forget(id) when it sinks; Wakes holds 16 ships × 43-point ribbons laid on the sphere and displaced by the swell, one veil draw (1,344 triangles): the bow wave and Kelvin-wedge arms in lead-white dry brush, the churned track, transverse waves and the arms' edges in broken ink, world-anchored, fading over WAKE_LIFE (14 s). Territory hook (WP20): Sea.setTerritory(cube) — a cubeTexture in seaFieldFaces' layout, RGBA bytes: rgb the owner's wash transmittance (white unowned), a 1 owned; composited after the depth washes as the owner's glaze with the style's pooled, granulating edge on the 0.5 contour of a, a narrow hatched band inside and the border inked in the owner's pigment. Currents (WP22): the 16 fixed lanes drawn as fast ink streaks and foam flecks, foam along their flanks and a faint glaze; Sea.setStreams(arcs) takes up to 8 more arcs with the same look (the Great Current's stretch in view); its glow is WP22's. Richness: wave strokes 2.4× denser with doubled marks and a second, larger crest family; the swell's backs hatched along the crests; lace foam over reefs and in the shore's wash; drift lines along the coast. Kelp and coral (flora.ts veil) now sit at their apparent depth under the water, depth-tested as the surface over them.

Remaining: (1) the game sea's wind: windAt throws in the shell's frames (shelterAt reads .mechanics of an undefined region), so createSea keeps the default wind; once WP6 fixes that, call Sea.setWind(foot, wind.v, wind.strength) there. (2) GPU time of the richer fragment on the throttled iPad (WP30 check 6); the floor and mirror branches are skipped over deep water and in fair weather. (3) Floor and plane-drawn marks thin out in a narrow band along the cube's diagonals (no seam; the washes run on). (4) view=curve frames only sky in this capture; check the view.

Scope: The sky as a painted wash deepening to the zenith; clouds outlined in ink and shaded by hatching, drifting with the wind; the sun a gold-leaf disc with drawn rays; each hour's hand-picked palette (dawn rose/pale gold, midday clear ultramarine, golden hour amber/vermilion, blue hour violet); night drawn as by candlelight (indigo washes, silver-ink highlights, gold-leaf stars, silver moon, warm lantern and lighthouse glows; every ship and coast readable); the shared light registry and lighting uniforms for every material; lighthouse beam primitive (turning at night); GLSL hooks for aurora and bioluminescence used by WP16/WP17.

WP15 — Terrain, lighthouses, harbours and flora

Status: done (2026-10-24) — part A (terrain, log), part B (lighthouses and harbours, log), WP15d (terrain richness, log) and part C (the shared flora, log); follow-ups under Remaining. Deps: WP3, WP13, WP14. Parallel-safe with: WP8.

Scope: island terrain meshes from geography (quadtree LOD adapted from legacy; no seams, pinching or floating); ink/hatch/wash land materials driven by region palettes (cliff strata, beaches by sand colour, rock, ice, basalt, mud); coastline inking; lighthouses on headlands everywhere — well kept, warmly lit, beautiful, beams turning at night, of unknown origin, never ruined or spooky; harbours (≥ 1 per region) as natural sheltered moorings with no houses, villages or signs of people; the shared instanced flora system (pines, palms, broadleaf jungle, flowering trees, mangrove, grasses, kelp, coral) bending in the planetary wind, with LOD and culling; foliage driven by the wind field.

Owns: src/render/terrain.ts structures.ts flora.ts, tests/render/terrain.*.

DoD: shots of a generic island from deck/high/orbit at three hours, a lighthouse at dusk and at night with beam, a harbour (wp15-*.png); budget numbers for a full region in view.

Built (part A, API for B and C). src/render/terrain.ts: TerrainField(geo) — corner(f, i, j) (a cell corner's height: the mean of the distinct cells round it, the same bits from every face at cube edges and corners), dir, normal, height(p) (the finest mesh's own triangles), surface(p, tide, out) → Surface {height (never below the sea), outward normal, up (n·up), ground, region}: B stands towers and moorings with it, C plants by its ground and slope; groundAt(region, height, up) with GROUND (beach, mud, meadow, scree, rock, cliff, ice, basalt) and SLOPES (the shader blends across the same thresholds); sandOf(region) (white, gold, black, mud, ice, shingle from shore.sand; coral sand is white); landformOf(region) (strata; basalt columns where a region has stacks or columns; ice where polar). buildChunks, template(level, mask), Terrain(pipeline, geo, swell) {mesh, field, chunks, triangles, bytes, update(camera, pxHeight)} and createTerrain, registered in MODULES after the sea (it takes the sea's swell from the new seas map in sea.ts). Mesh: 32-cell chunks (376 hold land; 10.2 MB of vertex and index buffers; built in ~100 ms); a level per chunk (33 … 3 vertices along an edge) from horizon and frustum culls and screen error (4 render px, spacing ≤ 48 px, hysteresis 0.7), neighbours kept within one level, T-junctions closed by snapping the finer rim's odd vertices onto the coarser line; one index buffer, one draw call, no textures of its own. Waterline: the land is never laid below the sea's surface; the fragment reads the sea's field cube, discards where the sea has water and inks the inner half of the sea's coast line (polygon offset; drawn before the sea). Material: glazes of the region's land pigment (rock; strata bands of uneven load with inked bedding lines), foliage (uplands), sand (wetter in the tide band; two tide lines and grains; ripples on mud), hexagonal basalt joints, stippled scree, ice glazed ultramarine; washes pooled along the shore; shade and sun glazes; hatching along the slope's contours (true height, straightened on the flats by the region's chart plane) deepening to cross-hatching; silver hatching by night; linework fades with the composite's distance. Hull-down by real geometry, as for the sea. Test page: tests/pages/sea.html now draws the terrain (new view orbit; region=, off=); node tests/pages/terrain.shots.ts retakes the shots. tests/render/terrain.test.ts: corners shared across cube edges and corners, templates tile and close T-junctions at every level and mask, every land cell chunked with mutual neighbours, queries follow the mesh.

Budget (A9), the Spice Isles from orbit (the whole region and its neighbours in view): terrain 1 draw call and 47 k triangles (iPad; 33 k iPhone), high 67 k / 54 k, deck 53 k; whole frame 6–11 calls, 101–151 k triangles; textures 13.7 MB (the terrain adds none) + targets 86.7 MB iPad / 57.8 MB iPhone ≤ 150 MB; terrain buffers 10.2 MB.

Built (part B). src/render/structures.ts (createStructures in MODULES after the terrain; finds the sky and terrain through the new skies/terrains maps): layLighthouses(geo, field) → Tower per geo.lights (seated on TerrainField.surface, stood back ≤ 4 u from a cliff's edge, plinth bedded below the lowest ground; lantern → Beams.seat(i, pos), new in light.ts, so the WP14 beam and lamp turn and glow from it); layHarbours(geo, field) → Mooring per geo.harbours {pos, mouth, r, lamp, rocks, bollards, posts}; Structures(style, geo, field, lights, beams) {towers, harbours, update(eye), triangles}. WP15d — terrain richness (2026-10-24, the user's art direction). carve(geo) → TerrainField.relief, each cell's drawn height: the geography's to the bit at or below KEEP (highest tide + 0.25 u), so the sim's coast and navigability are untouched (tested); above it carved by reliefOf(region) (amplitude, sheer, crag, gully, terrace, bench, from cliff height, landform, sand and climate): a shore profile (cliffed headlands alternating with slopes running down to coves), wave-cut benches at cliff feet, a domain-warped ridged field (hills, saddles, peaks) and inland massifs, cell-scale outcrops, gullies cut down the fall line (noise stretched along the coast distance's gradient), strata and columnar terracing, then tall land eased (e·0.85/(1+e/40)) so orbit reads as a planet with islands. Corners average carved cells, so cube edges and poles stay exact; structures and flora see it through surface(). Shader: sub-cell ridged relief lit through a surface-gradient bump (octaves fade before aliasing); beds bent by folds, tilted by a per-region dip, offset at faults, with staggered joints and fissures; hatching along the beds; cliff brow and foot inked heavier near the eye; talus fans stippled with boulders; outcrops, shingle pebbles, wind ripples, volcanic crust cracks, ice striae and crevasses; moss, lichen, dry ground and vegetated slopes in the washes, pooling at rock edges; sheer walls keep the mesh's own foot (no teeth along the field's waterline; ≤ 1 cell). Built (part C, API for WP16/WP17). src/render/flora.ts (createFlora in MODULES after the structures; floras map): FLORA/KINDS — 14 species (the regions' Flora plus reed), each with near/far kits, size, LOD distances and sway; kitGeometry(kind, lod) and floraMaterial(style, swell, {band, sway, far, veil}) for hero plants (instanced; per-instance aFoliage aBloom aSeed); plantBed(geo, field) → Bed (26757 plants in ~0.27 s, sorted into ~870 patches; same every match, tested); Flora.update(camera) refills one instanced draw per species and level from the patches in view (horizon, widened frustum, distance) only when the eye moves ≥ 1.2 u or turns ≥ 5°; Flora.stats. Drawn: lobed leaf masses with crescent leaf marks, contour hatching and shade glaze, per-instance pigments from the region palette (blossom in the accent), trunks in ink; wind from the swell's uWindAxis/uWind, sway ∝ height², gusts travelling downwind, leaf flutter; levels cross-fade by a world-anchored complementary stipple and far plants thin one by one (no popping). WP16 — Regions I: Ice Crown, Fjords, Basalt Stacks, Kelp Shallows, Amber Shoals, Weeping Isle

Status: done (2026-10-24) — parts A (Ice Crown, Fjords), B (Basalt Stacks), C (Kelp Shallows), D (Amber Shoals) and E (Weeping Isle) done; open follow-ups are each part's Remaining. Deps: WP15. Parallel-safe with: WP17 (or WP9).

Scope (each with its pigments, landforms, life habitats and one landmark that is the most beautiful thing in the region and reads clearly from orbit): Ice Crown — pack ice, tilted bergs, blue-white and silver inks, floes rotating with the sim, seals' floes, the Crown Light on its ice-bound skerry, aurora ribbons of verdigris and rose madder at night. Fjords — sheer cliffs streaked with falls, dark pine, deep green water, mist, the Great Falls. Basalt Stacks — hexagonal columns, sea arches, drifting fog banks, the Arch. Kelp Shallows — golden-green kelp forests swaying under clear water, rocky islets, the Kelp Cathedral, ships half-hidden in kelp. Amber Shoals — tidal flats and sandbars in warm gold light, rippled sand, the visible tide, the Sandbar Light. Weeping Isle — one towering cloud-forest mountain with cascades falling into the sea all round it.

Owns: src/render/regions/iceCrown.ts fjords.ts basaltStacks.ts kelpShallows.ts amberShoals.ts weepingIsle.ts.

WP17 — Regions II: Coral Atolls, Spice Isles, Fire Archipelago, Mangrove Labyrinth, Glass Sea, Maelstrom

Status: parts A, B built (2026-10-25); part A done — the Coral Atolls (log), S-017 fixes in part (48ef503); part B (Spice Isles) built (log); parts C–F (Fire Archipelago, Mangrove Labyrinth, Glass Sea, Maelstrom) not started. Deps: WP15. Parallel-safe with: WP16 (or WP10).

Fire Archipelago — smoking volcano, lava meeting the sea in steam, black sand, falling ash, the Caldera erupting on its rhythm (gold rain visual hook for WP22). Mangrove Labyrinth — arching roots, shaded green channels, still water, the Root Gate. Glass Sea — vast calm mirror under open sky, glowing blue-green bioluminescence at night tracing wakes and whales, the Whale Road. Maelstrom — great whirlpool, spiral currents, spray, a sky that turns with it, the Eye.

Owns: src/render/regions/coralAtolls.ts spiceIsles.ts fireArchipelago.ts mangroveLabyrinth.ts glassSea.ts maelstrom.ts.

WP18 — The shipwright

WP19 — Ships alive: motion, sails, damage, sinking, fittings

Status: done (2026-10-24) — part A: motion on the swell, heel, sails that tell the truth, pennants, leader, wakes, spray, lanterns, damage states with smoke and fire, levels; part B: the sinking, every fitting and the wounds built into the hull, struck colours, canvas to leeward on every tack and rigging clear of canvas (both tested), hero close-ups (wp19b-*). Deps: WP9, WP13, WP18. Parallel-safe with: WP11.

Scope: Being a ship — heel into turns and under a press of sail, lift on swells, bow through chop, surge on gusts; sails tighten when eager, shiver when hit, hang slack and torn when badly damaged; sails always show the truth (full and drawing on a reach, flattened close-hauled, billowing forward on a run; reefed/plain/full); pennants streaming; the leader's gold-leaf pennant seen from far; stern lanterns brighter in battle, guttering when the hull is low; damage (holes, splintered rails, smoke, fire, deepening list); sinking (roll, fill, slip beneath, pennant last, the sea closing in rings of ink); fittings visible on the body (copper sheathing glint, ram bow, cutwater, swivel guns on the rails, long guns, stern chasers, mortar, spare canvas, lateen mizzen, storm sails, doubled planking, pumps, crow's nest, stern glass); immediate cosmetic response to the helm; ship readability by silhouette at iPhone arm's length.

Owns: src/render/ships/shipView.ts (+ sails.ts if split), tests/render/shipView.*.

DoD: shots/sequence of each point of sail, damage stages, a full sinking sequence, each fitting visible (wp19-*.png); no rigging/sail clipping during animation.

Built, part A (2026-10-24; API for part B, WP22, WP23, WP24). src/render/ships/shipView.ts: Fleet, the render module (createShips, appended to world.ts MODULES), fleets.get(scene) → {views[i] (ShipView by sim index, null until seen), plumes, group, triangles}; it reads sim windAt/skyAt per ship, EVENT hit/ram/struck per tick, feeds seas.get(scene).wakes, registers each lantern in skies.get(scene).lights, runs fleetLods once a frame. ShipView(style, id, simShip) (form from s.form, pigments TEAMS[s.pigment], emblem from s.pattern: 1… in EMBLEMS order, 31 the player's gull, 0 plain): update(s, frame, Conditions, rider) interpolates was/pos and wasHead/head at alpha, rides Swell.ride (heave, pitch, roll), heels on a spring (MOTION: 0.17 rad to leeward at fair wind/full sail/beam on, 0.05 rad outward per u/s² of turn, a 0.26 rad list as the hull goes, cap 0.45; spring 10/s², damp 3.4/s, so the helm shows within half a second), surge and chop pitch, settles 0.55·draught·lost²; sails: the sail step eases set/reef (1.4/s) between rig.state canvases, fill by point of sail on the apparent wind (35 % of own way: flat close-hauled, full on a reach, billowing on a run, luffing and shivering head to wind), shiver 1 on a hit fading 1.6/s, torn under chain shot (s.torn) or with hull loss, slack in a calm or a wreck; then trim, setRig, setWind (pennants on the apparent wind), setLeader. Lantern: a Lamp (6 u, ×1.5 reloading or ablaze, guttering past 60 % hull lost) and the wood uLantern glow. exhale(plumes, …): smoke while ablaze (and thin smoke when battered) rising and drifting downwind, flame tongues, bow spray at speed (more as the bow plunges and in a squall). perch(out): the masthead truck for WP23's gull. Readable: at, up, forward, heel, pitch, lost, rig, lamp, ship (WP22 hangs muzzle flashes and hits on ship; WP24 chases at/forward). Plumes(style): lobed smoke billows (inked rim thinning with age, umber wash, hatched shade), vermilion/ochre flame teardrops, lead-white spray flecks; one veil draw, 480 puffs. Budgets (A9, ten ships, tests/render/shipView.shots.ts): whole frame ≤72 draws and ≤376 k triangles (chart view); ships ≤47 draws and ≤78 k triangles drawn (fleetLods submits 121 k, culled included) on iPad, 33–41 draws and 17–52 k on iPhone. Shots wp19-{deck,chart,storm,night,damaged}-{ipad,iphone}.png, sheets wp19-sheet-{ipad,iphone}.png (node tests/render/shipView.shots.ts; page tests/render/shipView.html?scene=). Test tests/render/shipView.test.ts (interpolated position/heading, heel to leeward and in a turn, sails per point of sail and step, damage states, levels, no NaN, budgets).

Built, part B (2026-10-24). src/render/ships/outfit.ts: Outfit builds the sim's six fitting slots (s.fittings, all 20 FITTING kinds: stem post, iron ram with a gilt collar, cutwater; light guns at the rails, long guns run out, swivels on posts, chain-shot racks, the shot furnace (smoking); stern chasers, powder kegs, mortar; spare canvas, a lateen yard with its sail furled, tarred storm canvas; copper at the waterline, doubled iron-bolted strakes, chain pumps (spouting once the hull is low); stern glass and quarter lights, a crow's nest with a hand in it, a lookout with a gilt glass; tier ≥ 3 gilds) and the ship's Wounds (shot holes ringed with splinters, rails stove in, scorching after fire, fresh planks nailed over holes once repaired) with the shipwright's mesher in its wood zones, merged into the hero and mid wood: 0 extra draw calls, ≤ 3.3k triangles (a galleon with six fittings and 12 wounds: 46.3k at the hero level, budget 56k); hero ships carry four hands and coils of rope in ink; dressHull(m, rig, fittings, wounds, fine, seed, crew). shipView.ts: SINK, timed from the sim's sink (afloat → sunk): colours struck and the list deepening to 0.75 rad by 1.2 s, canvas taken in and spray where the rig goes by the board at 3.2 s (spars adrift), down by the stern (even ids) or the bow to 0.6 rad from 5.5 s, the masthead pennant last under at 10.5 s; five rings of ink, a slick and ~20 pieces of flotsam left on the sea, the lantern drowned, the wake left to fade; removed at 11 s (v.gone). Built (API for WP25/WP27/WP31). src/render/camera.ts CameraRig (= WorldView.rig): zoom: Zoom (the lift: 0..1 deck pinch low→high, 1..2 the chart zoom z; open(), close(), set(lift), chartOpen, z, reducedMotion; critically damped, monotonic, reversible mid-move), pinch: Pinch (touch(t) returns true when two fingers took it — fingers closing lift toward the chart; the match screen forwards every touch, one finger stays WP25's), focus (a Vector3 gameplay wants framed, else the nearest rival in sight), preset('deckLow'|'deckHigh'|'midPull'|'orbit'|'chart') for captures, layout(view) (safe area); zoomPhases(z), DECK_LOW/DECK_HIGH/ORBIT_DIST. src/render/chart.ts: Net (layout(root), pose(u), toNet, fromNet, unproject(x, y, camera, view) → the sphere direction under a finger on the open chart, null off the net: course drawing and the edge tap), Chart (layer 1; drawn ship bits), chartShips(state, sight), geographyCube(world), groundOf(world). Built (part B). camera.ts: Zoom.step caps the lift in log-altitude (ZOOM_LOG_RATE 4.2/s, about 7 % of the eye's height a frame at 60 fps) through the deck pinch, the rise (with the swing north) and the flatten/unroll pan. The replay's 156 % frame step was the zoom itself (closing from orbit stepped z by 0.058 a frame where the altitude is steepest), not the sim; the replay test now passes at a worst 9.1 % (bound 10 %). Game watches the Voyage each tick and keeps an Outcome; results() is the ResultsSheet, whose “The next voyage” turns to the nine captains nearest the new rank with their cards and emblems, then “To harbour”. Home from a voyage that brought the chart something new, the harbour opens on the chart page and inks it in: the new seas bloom as a ragged watercolour in the order entered, then each toLetter name is lettered with a quill cue (reduced motion: drawn done). The chart draws the world’s own coastlines (Geography heights, marching squares: inked where seen, dashed where not, land washed, shoals dotted), the lighthouses and harbours seen, wrecks and caches found; names are cleared from the lines beneath them and sit over the rose; anchors stay on the plate. The timeline adds break-off, thrown, home, pennant and fights. Chest cards engrave their fittings at card scale (the gun on its carriage, the plated hull, the banded ram, mast and course, the glass); the claimed-sea tally is a hatched patch. WP28 — Audio I: engine and soundscape

Status: part A done (2026-10-24); part B remaining. Deps: WP5, WP9. Parallel-safe with: WP27 (any UI/render WP).

Scope: audio starts on the first touch; everything synthesized: wind in the rigging rising with speed, the hull's creak, the bow wave, canvas filling and flogging, rain, surf on reefs, ice grinding, steam hissing at the volcano, the Maelstrom's roar; gull calls, puffins, seals, whale song; cannon, splashes, splinters, fire, the long groan of a sinking; coins with rising pitch as they stream in, the loop-closing chord, the chest landing on deck; spatial mix and buses; sound/music volume settings. Adapt legacy synthesis.

Owns: src/audio/engine.ts sfx.ts, tests/audio/* (offline-render tests: deterministic buffers, no clipping, CPU per frame).

DoD: offline renders of each sound saved as waveform plots (PNG) in progress/shots/wp28-*.png (no audio files committed); CPU budget measured.

Done (part A): src/audio/dsp.ts (seeded noise, RBJ biquads, modal resonators, FM, plucked strings, envelopes, loop crossfades); sfx.ts 62 sounds / 132 variants, all layered synthesis, deterministic by name+variant: own ship (creak, block, canvas fill/flog, bow slap), combat (cannon, distant rolling boom, whistle, splash, thud on land, hit/splinters, ram, reef scrape, ignite, fire crackle loop, sinking groan, keg/shell explosion, mortar), reward (coin, chest landing, chest open by pigment tier 0-4, bank, loop chord sized 0-3 in D, trail broken, pennant), life (gull call, kek, wings, dive, whale, seal, puffin), ice grind, eruption, thunder, bell, consumables (patch, ward, fog flask, gust, flare), current rush, UI (tap, page, quill, confirm, back) and 11 seamless stereo beds (sea, swell, surf, wind, rigging whistle, rain, storm wall, Maelstrom, night crickets, harbour, steam); bankWorker.ts renders the bank off the main thread (beds and commonest first; 2-5 s of one core on the dev Mac); engine.ts buses sfx/ambience/ui/music each through a duck stage, glue compressor, limiter and a soft-clip ceiling 0.97; listener on the sphere (inverse distance from 2 u, gone by 95 u, pan by the ear’s right, air absorption and horizon occlusion as low-pass, travel delay at 30 u/s, far broadsides switch to the rolling boom); voice caps per sound and per pool (sfx 24, ambience 14, ui 4), stealing by priority×level over the whole scheduled interval; unlock on first touch (silent sample inside the gesture, stays armed for interruptions), suspend/resume with the app; soundscape.ts beds from wind/gust, sky (squall, roughness), the storm wall’s distance, coast (aground sampled at 3/7/12 u), Maelstrom, hour; own-ship life (creak with sea and heel, slaps with speed, flogging into the wind, fill+block on a sail change), thunder in squalls, coastal gulls, harbour bells; every EVENT kind mapped and placed, fires follow burning ships. src/app/game.ts holds GameAudio, hears every tick’s events after each step, frames it, suspends/resumes it. Tests: tests/audio/sfx.test.ts (Node: finite, peak 0.2-1, loudness -36..-4 dBFS, lengths, determinism, variants differ, seamless loops, click-free tails, bank cost), tests/audio/engine.test.ts (Chromium OfflineAudioContext through the full graph: every sound under the ceiling, ten-ship broadside storm over all beds holds the voice cap with no clipping, audio-thread render < 250 ms per s of audio in offline Chromium (measured 120-200 with other suites running), < 500 µs main thread per sound started, every event kind; WP28_SHOTS=<dir> writes a 93 s demo reel WAV there and the sheet progress/shots/wp28-sounds.png).

API (WP25/26/27/29): game.audio.engine: play(name, {at, gain, rate, variant, delay, pan, priority, duration}); ui('tap'|'page'|'quill'|'confirm'|'back'); chestOpen(tier), coins(n), broadside(at, guns), chord(size); loop/setVoice/stopVoice; onCue(c => …) gives {sound, level, when, near} for every sound as heard, for WP25 to pair haptics; setVolumes({sound, music}) for WP26 settings; duck(bus, db, hold); buses.music, clock (MusicClock: bpm, origin, beat(), next(div)) and key (loop chord root, Hz) for WP29; setListener(pos, fwd) for the camera (MatchAudio defaults it to the player’s ship).

Remaining (part B): listener on the real camera and occlusion from its eye height; region-placed life (puffins on stacks, seals on ice, whale song in the Glass Sea, otters, herons, dolphins at the bow) and ice grinding by floes, steam at the Caldera, the harbour bed at real harbours (regions are local coordinates: needs a world-space query); nearby rivals’ own creak/canvas; space (fog damping, caldera echo); on-device A14 listening and CPU pass, iOS interruption/route-change handling; the storm test’s audio-thread cost measured realtime on device.

WP30 — Performance, startup and budgets (check 6)

Status: in progress — WP30a (GPU performance foundation) part 1 recorded 2026-10-24 (log), part 2 (WP30b) 2026-10-24 (log): targets not yet met; check 6 not started. Deps: WP13–WP27. Parallel-safe with: WP29 only (it may touch any render file).

WP30a — GPU timing (M1 Pro, Chromium ANGLE/Metal, ipad 2752×2064, render scale pinned 1; medians, ms). Harness: node tests/render/gpu.timing.ts [--label= --scenes= --frames= --out=] drives __weather.profile() (tests/render/weather.page.ts): a timer query round every renderer.render of a frame; on ANGLE/Metal each query reads from the start of the command buffer (why WP2 found them "high"), so a pass is the difference of consecutive queries; a group is the scene pass with it hidden subtracted (±1–2 ms noise, negative where it hides sea). Every other group (terrain, flora, structures, ships, sky, territory, trails, interface) ≤ 2.1 ms, mostly < 1. Changes: a ¼-resolution mask pass (25 bilinear corner taps per 4×4 block) lets the composite skip its 29-tap gather away from lines; the sea draws wave strokes, caps and lane marks only where they can show and hatches territory only inside it; glaze returns at zero density; veils use paperPlain (paper without the marks' six taps). Look: the five full 2752×2064 frames before vs after differ by at most 1/255 (0 % of pixels > 2/255; the same frame 1 s apart: 0) — crops progress/shots/wp30a-{before,after}-{sea,bruise,calm,fog,inside}.png (600×400 at 1:1). Dynamic resolution unchanged and frame-time steered (WKWebView has no timer queries): two slow frames in ~0.5 s drop a 5 % step, a rise needs 10 s calm and a failed rise bars that scale 60 s; floor 0.6 (1.2 device px per CSS px on the 2× iPad); interface at native resolution. Scaling: A14 GPU ≈ 1/5.5 of the M1 Pro, A14 iPads 2360×1640 (0.68× the pixels) ⇒ A14 ≈ 3.7× these ms at scale 1, ≈ 1.35× at the 0.6 floor: the 7/9 ms targets would hold 60 fps at scale ≈ 0.75. Remaining to ≤ 7 clear / ≤ 9 storm: the sea fragment (6–10 ms: a far-sea path skipping floor, marks, foam and drift with explicit-gradient taps; the six world-anchored triplanar noise taps packed into one; the four depth washes as one transmittance product; mesh LOD against horizon overshading); weather veil and spray (1–4 ms) and the storm (4.4 ms) folded into the composite as one depth-based veil instead of blended full-screen shells; scene-pass base cost.

WP30b — sea fragment gating, all exact (frames identical to before: max 1/255, 0 % of pixels > 2/255 on sea, calm, inside; crops progress/shots/wp30b-{before,after}-{calm,sea}.png, 600×400 at 1:1 from the centre): the floor's two cube taps only within the 110 u where it can show; each floor's cells, hatching and marks (rock, coral, kelp) only where that floor lies; foam's and drift's six tMarks taps only where foam or drift can show (triGrad in glsl.ts: explicit gradients, so in a branch); the marks' coarser octave only where it blends in. Harness: --quantile=0.1 (each pass's 10th percentile) and gpu().p10, for a loaded machine. Measured as the HEAD tree (a scratch worktree) and the edited tree interleaved twice while other agents loaded the machine (load average 11–29, 30+ Chromium processes: every pass ran 1.3–1.6× its quiet part-1 time, so these ms do not compare with the table above). WP33 — Review R1 (fresh harsh critic)

Status: not started. Deps: WP31, WP32. Parallel-safe with: nothing (reviewer works alone).

Scope: PROMPT.md "Review": an independent reviewer with fresh context that has not seen the code, the conversation or any earlier review. It receives only PROMPT.md, the captures and the running game, which it plays through scripted touch on the harness. It judges every capture against PROMPT.md and the play by playing, and reports every flaw — style breaks (realistic shading, screen-space textures, inconsistent line, off-palette colour), unreadable frames, team colours blurring, UI over the action, distorted geometry, seams or pinching, floating/intersecting objects, rigging clipping sails, border flicker, trail gaps, broken animation, camera problems, text not in the generated hand or illegible, any human form or settlement, slow/vague/unfair controls, stupid/unfair/indistinguishable captains, dragging or abrupt matches, unclear losses, console errors and warnings.

Owns: Review findings only (no code). DoD: every finding recorded with id, severity, where, evidence screenshot.

WP34+ — Fix rounds, then review again until clean

Status: not started. Deps: the preceding review.

Scope: group the findings by domain into fix packages F1a, F1b, … each sized like a WP and owning disjoint files (at most two in parallel); each fix re-takes the affected captures, re-runs the affected checks, and records "how it was fixed" against each finding. Then R2: a new reviewer with fresh context reviews again; repeat F2, R3, … The game is done when a fresh reviewer finds nothing to fix and every acceptance check passes.
